#5223. Nvidia Slides 5% Ahead of High-Stakes Independent Spy Probe

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Steve is a crypto news writer with a passion for decoding market moves. He blends breaking blockchain news with sharp technical analysis and bold price predictions. From Bitcoin rallies to altcoin breakouts, Steve breaks it all down with clarity and insight. Whether you're a trader or just curious, his analysis keeps you ahead of the curve.

Nvidia has hired external counsel to lead investigations into the recent Chinese espionage allegations involving CEO Jensen Huang.

The probe will be led by the famous investigator Joon H. Kim and his prestigious firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen and Katz.

NVDA stock has slid 5.47% in the day, closing at $168.89.

Congress has summoned Huang to discuss the matter, while the FBI conducts its own probe.

Independent members of the Nvidia board have now retained former acting US Attorney Joon H. Kim and his law firm, Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen and Katz, to conduct a thorough and independent probe into the Chinese spy scandal involving co-founder and CEO Jensen Huang.

On Sunday, the Nvidia board developed an independent committee to investigate whether Huang’s extramarital affair compromised corporate and/or national security. The committee concluded zero data breach, with the board expressing continued confidence in his leadership role in the company.

However, in their latest action, the committee hired top prosecutor Kim to lead an external investigation into the matter. Kim is a formidable American attorney, with one of his most prolific cases being the Governor Andrew Cuomo sexual harassment investigation of 2021. His current employer, Wachtell Limpton, is arguably the most prestigious and feared “crisis management” firm on Wall Street.

By seeking an external probe, the Nvidia board is pursuing a detached and transparent approach to the matter rather than executing a corporate cover-up. This insulates members from personal legal liability, and further shareholder lawsuits and Congressional demands.

A new statement from the independent members of the NVIDIA Board: https://t.co/uSFDhiohG8

On Monday, retail investor Mark Kistenmacher filed a class action lawsuit representing all investors who purchased NVDA shares between October 9, 2025 and November 8, 2026. The lawsuit demands unspecified compensatory damages from Nvidia, arguing that the firm artificially inflated its stock price by downplaying Jensen’s interactions with the former Megaspeed executive.

And while Trump has vouched for Jensen’s innocence, three House Republican Committee chairmen have summoned Huang for a closed-door Congressional briefing on the matter on November 18 (a day after its Q3 earnings report). While Jensen has pledged full cooperation with investigators, he is yet to confirm attendance for the briefing.

US lawmakers will likely propose bills to enforce tougher export rules, making it harder to sell chips to China. China, meanwhile, has called the spy allegations “unsubstantiated”, saying they are merely meant to taint its image.

Following Sunday’s disclosure, Nvidia stock wiped out over $200 million in market value, while rivals like AMD surged over 13%.

At the last market close, NVDA (Nasdaq) traded at $168.89, having declined a further 5.47% in the day.

While analysts expect a strong finance report on Tuesday, developments in the prevailing controversy could massively impact future stock prices since Huang is the face of the company.

#5222. Vitalik Buterin Backs an AI Slowdown as AI Tokens Slide 30%: Is the AI Crypto Bubble Popping?

Vitalik Buterin backs slowing the AI data center race. AI tokens fell 29.7% in 30 days, three times Bitcoin. Is the AI crypto bubble popping?

Vitalik Buterin said on Sunday that slowing the AI data center race is probably a good thing, as the crypto bubble around AI tokens loses air. The Ethereum co-founder made the case in a post on X.

He pushed back on what he called the “strong emotional reaction” against the anti-data-center movement.

I know this is a predictable opinion from me but I don't agree with the strong emotional reaction against the anti-data-center movements that I've seen this week.

As I see it, algorithmic progress means that whatever you would be able to do with more and bigger data centers in 2…

— vitalik.eth (@VitalikButerin) November 8, 2026

That movement had a big week. On November 3, voters in 10 states decided 41 local ballot measures on data centers, Ballotpedia counted. A “yes” vote on 28 of them would limit building through bans or moratoriums. After the vote, Senator Bernie Sanders called for a “national data center moratorium now,” one of several Democrats pushing to rein in the AI buildout.

Crypto’s AI corner has felt the same chill. Seven of the largest AI tokens fell 29.7% on average over the past 30 days, almost three times Bitcoin’s 10.7% drop.

So is the slowdown Buterin wants already showing up as a popping bubble in AI tokens?

Discover: Bitcoin Slides to $73,510 Over the Weekend: Did Nvidia Scandal Trigger BTC’s $2,000 Flash Crash?

His case rests on three points, and he made it, in his words, “without any controversial ‘doomer arguments’.”

He concluded that “a slowdown in compute hyper-scaling seems quite net good.” Even if it proved a mistake, it would not be worse than “10+ other political things,” he added.

The view fits what Buterin has argued since 2023, when he set out “d/acc,” his name for speeding up defensive technology first, in an essay titled My techno-optimism.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

AI tokens are the part of crypto that trades most directly on the AI boom. They include networks that sell computing power, run AI agents or pay people to train models. CoinGecko valued the sector at $18.9 billion on Monday morning.

We compared the 30-day moves of seven of the largest AI tokens on Binance with Bitcoin and Ether.

The selling lines up with doubts about AI spending in the stock market. Last Thursday’s sell-off in chip stocks pushed Bitcoin below its 200-day average, and AI tokens, which rose fastest on the AI story, have fallen fastest as it cools.

A wave of local data center bans adds a new worry for tokens that sell computing power. Buterin’s own argument cuts the other way, though: if better algorithms keep AI improving on today’s machines, a slower buildout does not end demand for AI.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

It held on Sunday, but with little room to spare. Ether trades at $2,104 on Binance, down 16.1% in 30 days, after touching $2,060 late Sunday, its lowest price since August 19.

ETH sits below both of its daily exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that weight recent prices more. They stand at $2,311 for 200 days and $2,370 for 50 days.

For traders asking whether the crypto bubble is bursting, AI tokens are the place to watch. They rose fastest on the AI story, and they are now falling fastest.

#5221. XRP Whales Move $900 Million in Biggest Inflow Since 2024

XRP's price correction has continued to deepen, with the leading altcoin retesting its multi-month low around $1.08, causing trading activity to intensify, especially among large holders.

Latest onchain data from crypto analytics platform CryptoQuant shows that large XRP holders are moving an unusually high volume of tokens to Binance, stirring speculation about increased selling activity among whales.

Notably, the data shows that whales have deposited about 691 million XRP into Binance within 24 hours, marking the largest single-day inflow recorded since 2024.

While XRP was trading around $1.3 during the period, the total XRP that flowed into the exchange that day was worth about $900 million, drawing attention from market analysts.

The massive XRP inflow has stirred panic among investors about the asset's potential performance, as it has surpassed the previous inflow record seen during a major market correction in February 2025.

It is important to note that the surge is not limited to a single day, as XRP's 90-day moving average of daily whale inflows has also surged above $100 million following recent market declines.

As such, the metric has reached its highest level in nearly six months, suggesting that large holders have been moving increasing amounts of XRP to Binance over an extended period.

The massive increase in XRP whale inflows has come as XRP continues to face a severe market decline, falling far below its local lows.

Following the extensive price decline, XRP has just retested its multi-month low around $1.08, a price level last seen on August 20, sparking fear among traders.

Although whale inflows do not necessarily mean that the whales have sold their holdings, the coinciding price bloodbath points to increased selling activity.

#5220. “Tired After 7 Years”: Why This Veteran XRP Investor Considers Selling Everything at Current Prices

XRP’s potential run to $10 may not be enough to keep every longtime holder invested. In a striking admission, a long-time XRP investor has revealed plans to sell his entire position at current prices, despite the token’s much-anticipated upside target. The comments have sparked fresh debate over whether years of waiting will ultimately pay off for XRP holders or whether some are ready to walk away before the next parabolic rally.

In a recent post on X, Genfinity founder Ryan Solomon, known as King Solomon, expressed growing frustration with XRP after holding onto the cryptocurrency for seven years. Despite the community’s long-standing hopes of a major explosion toward $10, he suggested he may no longer be willing to wait for that milestone, revealing plans to sell his entire XRP position at current market prices and citing the significant opportunity cost of staying invested so long.

“When XRP eventually hits $10, many won’t sell,” Solomon said. “I’m likely selling everything at current prices. Tired after 7 years. Too much opportunity cost,” Solomon wrote.

He also expressed frustration with what he described as shifting narratives and gaslighting surrounding XRP. After years of waiting, he seems increasingly unconvinced that continuing to hold the payments-focused token is worth sacrificing other investment opportunities.

The comments carry added weight given Solomon’s years of involvement in researching XRP and the broader digital-asset infrastructure sector through Genfinity. His willingness to consider walking away after seven years suggests that even the prospect of XRP eventually reaching the much-coveted $10 level may no longer be enough to keep some longtime holders committed.

In simple words, his stance highlights the frustration that can build even among long-term participants in the XRP ecosystem. That sentiment comes as XRP hovers around $1.10, according to CoinGecko data, marking a decline of approximately 51% over the past year and highlighting a prolonged downturn that is testing investors’ patience.

Another longtime XRP community member going by the name Nikolas echoed Solomon’s frustration, admitting that holding out for the $5-$10 price range was one of his biggest investment mistakes. He also criticized the XRP community for what he described as a growing inability to think critically, suggesting that many holders have become too attached to bullish expectations.

The investor further questioned the relationship between Ripple Labs and XRP, arguing that the company’s business interests appear increasingly disconnected from the token’s performance. In his view, Ripple may be prioritizing its corporate growth and investors while XRP holders struggle to see comparable benefits from the company’s expansion.

Meanwhile, crypto commentator Good Evening Crypto suggested that Solomon’s concerns may reflect a much broader sentiment among longtime XRP holders. He said a friend working in private equity, who has held XRP since 2021, recently expressed similar frustrations about the opportunity cost of staying invested.

Although Good Evening Crypto said he does not share the same view, he acknowledged that Solomon’s decision to consider selling deserves attention given his experience and standing in the community. He suggested that thousands of other XRP holders may be quietly reaching the same conclusion but have yet to voice their frustrations publicly.

Together, these comments point to growing discontent among some longtime XRP holders, raising questions about whether the token’s anticipated upside still justifies the years of waiting.

#5218. 'I Sold 2.5 Million XRP': Ripple Vet David Schwartz Rebuts Critics With Tax Proof

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP Ledger co-creator David Schwartz dropped his $1.7 million tax bill on X to settle a long-running fight over the token’s value. Ripple’s CTO Emeritus was swatting away haters who accused him of dumping XRP on the market. One commenter called XRP “a terrible investment,” adding that nobody would blame the engineer for heading for the exit. Schwartz kept it short: “The $1.7 million I paid in capital gains tax says otherwise.”

In 2025, Schwartz sold about 2.5 million XRP, according to his own words. Nearly all of it came from bonuses for his work in 2020 and 2023. Posting the numbers was no accident. Schwartz is killing rumors about what Ripple’s "big guns" really pocket and proving he has nothing to hide.

The sale is one chapter in a bigger personal saga Schwartz has been unpacking piece by piece from 2024 through 2026. At his peak he held around 26 million XRP. By August 2026, that stack had shrunk to just 2 million. Describing the 92% drawdown, he chose his words carefully: “I think I’m down from about 26 million at my peak to about 2 million now.”

Some went out as gifts and transfers, but he sold most of it on the open market.

He started taking profits the moment the price touched $0.10. Schwartz admits he never believed the coin would break $0.25. The same paranoia made him dump his entire 40,000 ETH position at a measly $1.05 apiece.

“Obviously, I wish I hadn’t done those things,” the developer says of the billions he left on the table.

Schwartz opposes the approach many attribute to as "diamond hands". He openly says he “really, really hates risk” and doesn’t count himself among long-term holders. He and his wife had an ironclad rule to sell a fixed slice of their holdings every time the price printed a new all-time high. Cashing out is simply the cost of being able to “sleep better at night.” These days, volatility barely touches him as his net worth is now largely cut loose from price swings.

Based on his own admission, Schwartz moved most of his savings into Ripple stock. The shares are illiquid, sure, but they give direct exposure to the tech giant’s wins and fully insulate his personal capital from exchange chaos.

#5217. Top Five AI Crypto Projects to Watch as AI Adoption Grows

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Yash is a crypto analyst specializing in price analysis, predictions, and in-depth research reports. He combines technical indicators with on-chain data to uncover market trends and potential breakouts. His sharp insights help readers navigate the crypto market with confidence. Whether it’s Bitcoin or emerging altcoins, Yash breaks it down with clarity and precision.

Artificial intelligence is evolving beyond conversational tools into autonomous systems capable of conducting research, writing code, analyzing information, and completing complex workflows.

The release of Artificial Analysis’s dashboard’s metric known as ‘Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index v5’ highlights this progress through more demanding benchmarks, private evaluation datasets, and improved testing of real-world AI capabilities.

The findings reveal three important trends: leading AI models are becoming more capable, computational requirements are increasing, and significant performance differences remain between proprietary and open-weight models.

These developments are making a real difference in society and could create opportunities for AI crypto projects focused on decentralized computing, machine intelligence, and even autonomous AI agents.

Mong crypto sector, there are five best projects that stand out for their involvement in these areas: Bittensor (TAO), Render Network (RENDER), Akash Network (AKT), Artificial Superintelligence Alliance (FET), and Virtuals Protocol (VIRTUAL).

Their long-term potential will depend on real-world adoption, competitive infrastructure, and whether growing AI activity translates into sustainable demand for their tokens.

Released on October 27, 2026, Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index v5 introduced two new benchmarks and upgrades three existing evaluations.

Approximately 60% of the index now relies on private test sets or held-out answers, reducing the influence of familiarity with publicly available benchmark questions.

The index evaluates four categories, each carrying 25% of the overall weighting:

The overall rankings place Claude Opus 5.5 at 58 points, GPT-6 Astra at 57, and GPT-6.1 Sol at 55. Gemini 4 Argon follows at 50.

Performance varies across individual evaluations. Claude Opus 5.5 leads FrontierCode 2.0 at 46% and HLE-Diamond at 68%, while GPT-6 Astra leads Terminal-Bench-Science at 63% and the CritPt v2 hard subset at 72%. This demonstrates that no single model dominates every category.

Announcing Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index v5, featuring 2 new benchmarks and 3 upgrades. For the first time, the majority of the Index is built on private datasetsThe Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index is our composite measure of model capabilities across tasks… pic.twitter.com/oA4tsXQOC6

The strongest model without released weights, Kimi K3.1 Pro, scores 46 on the index. Among models with released weights, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017 and GLM-5.3 lead at 32.

The gap between the overall leader and the strongest open-weight model has increased from three points in version 4.3 to 12 points in version 5.

This presents a challenge for decentralized AI infrastructure, where access to competitive models is essential for delivering useful services.

One of the most important findings concerns the resources required to complete increasingly complex AI tasks.

Artificial Analysis reports that median output tokens per task increased from approximately 35,000 in version 4.3 to 59,000 in version 5, representing an increase of roughly 69%.

Agentic evaluations account for approximately 88% of median output-token usage despite representing 55% of the index’s weighting.

The cost per task also varies significantly, ranging from $0.96 for GPT-6.1 Terra to $13.04 for Claude Fable 5.1 among the ten highest-scoring models shown.

These findings highlight the growing importance of affordable and reliable computing infrastructure.

The Intelligence Index demonstrates that AI development is increasingly focused on completing practical tasks rather than simply generating responses.

Three developments are particularly relevant.

Coding and software development: FrontierCode 2.0 evaluates 132 tasks across real open-source repositories, measuring whether an AI agent produces changes that meet the requirements for a maintainer to merge them. Claude Opus 5.5 leads with 46%.

Scientific research: Terminal-Bench-Science evaluates 70 research tasks performed in a terminal environment. GPT-6 Astra leads at 63%, followed by Claude Opus 5.5 at 59%.

Knowledge and reasoning: HLE-Diamond evaluates 1,000 questions covering reasoning and knowledge. Claude Opus 5.5 leads at 68%, although knowledge questions remain more challenging than reasoning questions.

Together, these results through analyzing metrics clearly highlight the growing capabilities of agentic AI while demonstrating that accuracy, reliability, and computational efficiency remain important challenges.

As autonomous agents become more capable, demand could increase for computing resources, specialized AI services, data access, and automated transactions.

The development of advanced AI systems creates opportunities across several areas of the cryptocurrency ecosystem.

However, AI progress does not automatically translate into higher token prices. The opportunity depends on whether blockchain-based projects can attract genuine customers and compete with centralized alternatives.

Advanced AI models require substantial computing resources, particularly for inference, research, and autonomous agent execution.

Decentralized GPU marketplaces could provide alternative access to computing capacity while allowing hardware providers to monetize unused resources.

Render (RENDER) relies on a Burn-and-Mint Equilibrium (BME) model to balance computing demand with RENDER token burns, while Akash (AKT) leverages Cosmos SDK-based reverse auctions to lower provisioning costs. The success of these compute protocols hinges on real paid workload volume, network uptime, and GPU hardware availability rather than token speculation.

Autonomous AI agents require friction-free, trustless payment rails to purchase compute capacity, scrape dataset APIs, and execute cross-chain transactions without human intervention. Standard banking APIs and fiat payment rails cannot support self-custodial software execution.

Blockchain networks could support programmable payments and coordination between independent agents.

FET and Virtuals are relevant to this developing market. These provide the programmable micro-transaction layer via smart contracts and native tokens, allowing autonomous software entities to hold private keys, manage capital, and execute automated machine-to-machine payments.

However, conventional payment systems and centralized APIs will remain competitive alternatives.

Different AI models perform better at different tasks, creating opportunities for specialized intelligence services.

Bittensor attempts to coordinate independent contributors through subnet-based markets, rewarding participants for providing useful digital services.

Its long-term success depends on whether its incentive mechanisms reward genuine utility and attract sustainable external demand.

The most important distinction in evaluating AI crypto projects is the difference between product adoption and token demand.

A network may attract users without generating meaningful economic value for its token.

These factors provide a stronger basis for evaluating long-term potential than AI-related branding alone.

The following projects represent five different areas of the AI and blockchain ecosystem. They are selected for their technological relevance, not ranked by expected investment returns.

Category: Decentralized machine intelligence

Bittensor creates decentralized markets for digital commodities, including machine intelligence, computing, and other specialized services.

Its subnet architecture allows independent contributors to provide outputs, while validators evaluate performance and distribute incentives.

TAO is central to Bittensor’s incentive and network-security model. Its economic design connects token distribution with subnet participation, while validators evaluate the quality of contributions within individual subnets. Subnet-level incentives add another layer to this structure, making it important to examine how emissions are distributed and whether rewards reflect measurable utility. The key metric is whether subnet activity attracts external demand rather than depending primarily on token incentives.

Moreover, On-chain subnet emission data further illustrates the network’s incentive distribution, with SN51 currently receiving 17.07% of emissions, followed by SN64 at 9.34% and SN4 at 8.04%, highlighting the varying allocation of TAO incentives across leading subnets.

Why it matters: Bittensor offers an alternative approach to developing and coordinating AI services without relying entirely on centralized providers.

Bittensor uses its native consensus algorithm (Yuma Consensus) to rank and reward subnet contributors. Subnets act as competitive machine learning markets where miners compete by providing optimized model outputs, and validators score their performance.

TAO tokens are minted per block and allocated dynamically across subnets based on performance metrics, incentivizing open-source AI development outside corporate walled gardens.

Key risk: Subnet quality, incentive design, token emissions, and the ability to attract genuine customer demand.

Category: Decentralized GPU infrastructure

Render Network connects GPU providers with customers requiring computing resources. Its ecosystem extends beyond graphics rendering into selected AI-related applications, including machine learning, inference, and generative AI.

RENDER uses a burn-and-mint equilibrium model designed to connect network usage with token economics. In broad terms, tokens are burned in connection with rendering work, while network participants receive emissions-based rewards. This creates a potential link between paid workload demand and token supply dynamics.

The important factors to monitor are actual network usage, burn activity, reward emissions and whether demand for AI-related workloads becomes a meaningful part of network activity.

Moreover, Render’s cumulative burn has reached approximately 2.26 million RENDER as of November 6, 2026, providing an on-chain indicator of token consumption associated with network activity.

Why it matters: Growing AI workloads could increase demand for accessible GPU infrastructure and distributed computing services.

Key risk: Competition from centralized cloud providers and the need to demonstrate meaningful AI workload adoption.

Akash Network operates a marketplace for computing resources and has expanded into AI inference and GPU workloads through developments such as AkashML.

AKT plays a role in Akash’s decentralized marketplace economy, including network participation, staking and economic coordination.

Customers use the marketplace to obtain computing resources, while providers compete to supply infrastructure. The important economic question is how deployment payments, settlement processes, staking requirements and network fees translate into sustained AKT demand. Marketplace growth is more meaningful when it creates recurring economic activity rather than relying mainly on incentives.

Moreover, Akash Network’s reported GPU capacity has increased to 419, indicating growing available computing capacity within its decentralized GPU marketplace.

Why it matters: Developers may increasingly seek flexible and cost-effective infrastructure to deploy AI applications and autonomous agents.

Key risk: Infrastructure reliability, developer adoption, cloud-provider competition, and the relationship between network usage and AKT demand.

The Artificial Superintelligence Alliance brings together the Fetch.ai, Ocean Protocol, and SingularityNET ecosystems under an AI-focused initiative.

Its technology focuses on decentralized AI services, autonomous agents, and digital transactions.

FET is the ecosystem’s principal token for coordinating participation across its AI-focused infrastructure and services. Its long-term relevance depends on the extent to which agent-related services, network transactions, staking and other ecosystem activities require or create demand for the token. It is important to distinguish technical adoption of autonomous agents from actual FET usage. The token’s economic case becomes stronger when recurring services and transactions create measurable utility.

Moreover, The network’s transaction data records 36,429,122 total transactions, including 77,534 transactions over the past 30 days, offering a measurable view of recent on-chain activity.

Why it matters: Increasingly capable AI agents could create demand for systems that coordinate services, exchange information, and execute automated transactions.

Key risk: Agent adoption does not automatically translate into FET demand. Token utility and sustained commercial usage remain essential.

Virtuals Protocol focuses on creating and coordinating AI agents within tokenized digital ecosystems.

Its approach connects AI applications with economic mechanisms that allow agents and their users to participate in digital markets.

VIRTUAL is connected to the protocol’s AI-agent economy, where tokenized mechanisms support agent creation, participation and economic activity. Its token model should be assessed through the relationship between agent launches, transactions, liquidity mechanisms and recurring user demand. A growing number of agents does not necessarily mean sustainable token value capture. The key question is whether agents generate ongoing economic activity that requires VIRTUAL, rather than relying primarily on speculative trading or launch-related demand.

Moreover, The actively traded Virtuals agent market currently represents an estimated market capitalization of $337.27 million, based on the fully diluted valuation methodology for agents meeting the specified trading-volume threshold.

Why it matters: The growth of autonomous AI agents could create opportunities for platforms supporting specialized digital services and agent-based economies.

Key risk: Speculative activity, competition, limited recurring demand, and uncertainty around long-term token value capture.

Each project addresses a different requirement within the emerging AI economy. Their prospects will depend on product adoption, competitive positioning, token economics, and their ability to generate sustainable demand.

The Intelligence Index v5 findings point toward an AI industry increasingly focused on autonomous systems, complex workflows, and computationally intensive applications.

This could create opportunities for decentralized infrastructure and AI-focused blockchain networks.

Three developments are particularly important.

1. Growing demand for computing: More sophisticated AI applications could increase demand for GPUs and inference infrastructure, potentially benefiting Render and Akash.

2. Expansion of autonomous agents: AI agents may increasingly interact with digital services and execute transactions, creating opportunities for FET and Virtuals.

3. Specialized intelligence markets: Demand for task-specific AI services could support decentralized networks such as Bittensor.

However, centralized AI providers remain strong competitors. They offer established infrastructure, integrated services, and operational reliability.

There is also a downside scenario: AI adoption could accelerate without decentralized networks capturing meaningful economic activity.

Consequently, the future of AI crypto will depend less on technological narratives and more on real-world usage, competitive services, and sustainable token economics.

Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index v5 highlights the rapid development of AI capabilities, particularly in coding, scientific research, and autonomous workflows.

At the same time, the findings reveal increasing computational requirements and a significant performance gap between leading proprietary models and released open-weight alternatives.

These developments create potential opportunities for AI crypto projects involved in decentralized computing, specialized intelligence, and autonomous agents.

Bittensor, Render, Akash, Artificial Superintelligence Alliance, and Virtuals represent five different approaches to this emerging market.

Their long-term prospects will depend on whether they can convert technological relevance into real adoption and sustainable token demand.

The broader outlook for AI crypto is promising, but technological progress alone is not enough. The projects most likely to establish lasting positions will be those that solve genuine problems, attract paying users, and create measurable economic value through their networks.

#5216. Zcash Sets New Hashrate Record After NU7 Upgrade: What's Ahead?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Zcash has set a new high in its network hashrate just days after the NU7 upgrade, which slashed block times by a third to 25 seconds. Zcash activated its NU7 upgrade on November 6, 2026, at block 3,543,405.

Zcash network hashrate: 32.62 GSol/s, up 5.8% in 30 days. ⛏️ An all-time high. https://t.co/1vNROBAvd2

Zcash hashrate refers to the total computing power of all miners on the ZEC network. It is calculated by the current network difficulty, the average block find time set by the cryptocurrency network and/or the effective block find time of the latest blocks.

ZEC got 3x faster following the NU7 upgrade, which launched on November 6, with block times down from 75 to 25 seconds, and the Sprout pool retired, marking a new era for Zcash.

Faster blocks mean more room for shielded transactions. NU7 adds per-block limits: 330 Orchard or Ironwood actions, 300 Sapling inputs and outputs, a shared budget of 330 across pools, and no new Sprout JoinSplits. These limits more than double Orchard protocol throughput while reducing the worst-case sync load a spammer can push onto light wallets by about 37%.

NU7 does not raise Zcash's fixed 21 million cap; halvings will now come every 5,040,000 blocks instead of 1,680,000, so the issuance schedule holds at roughly four years per halving. NU7 also starts allocating funds for future mining rewards. Some 60% of aggregate transaction fees will flow into a public reserve that pays later mining rewards.

In what comes next after the NU7 upgrade, Zcash developers have proposed cutting block times in a potential NU7.1 upgrade just days after NU7 went live. The proposal would reduce target block times from 25 to 20 seconds, aiming to speed up transaction confirmations and improve user experience and network latency.

#5213. Ethereum (ETH) Gas Estimates Are Broken: Explaining the Fix and the Issue

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum transactions can appear successful, but there might be a problem hiding behind the scenes.

A Geth developer has proposed a fix to the client's gas estimation, according to draft pull request #36084 on GitHub. The patch addresses an issue in the current estimator, which could result in incorrect gas limits due to failed internal calls (calls from one smart contract to another) being ignored.

Although the transaction could still be completed, there is no evidence that the action the user wanted has actually happened.

The issue lies in a mechanism where a smart contract calls another smart contract, catches an error, and then proceeds to a refund, records the failure, or skips an item entirely. Technically, the transaction was successful, but the outcome is different for the user.

This could potentially result in wallets suggesting a gas limit that is enough to finish the transaction, but not enough to complete the work inside it. The developer also noted that EIP-8037 could make this behavior more common, since gas returned from failed internal execution can help fund the fallback.

According to the pull request, the change includes a stricter definition of success for gas estimates. This version of Geth would improve the way it deals with internal failures, counting failed internal calls and contract creations instead of checking only whether the transaction completes.

Even if the transaction completes, the estimate would get rejected if it produces more internal failures than a reference execution with a higher gas limit.

Nonetheless, the developer stressed that an internal call that always fails should not push the estimate higher indefinitely. The reference execution enables the estimator to keep existing failures without allowing additional ones caused by insufficient gas. The proposal also introduces a separate Amsterdam reference, preventing unnecessary increases made purely to access the state gas reservoir.

The developer states that they've replayed 432 mainnet transactions with the new set of rules from the Amsterdam update and that the suggested change fixed 64 out of 67 cases.

The gas limit increased by 4%. 254 additional estimates were unaffected due to the lack of the problem, with 3 cases being undetected.

For regular investors, the proposed change should improve the reliability of wallet estimates and reduce transactions that complete without delivering the intended result, despite introducing higher gas limits in some cases. Luckily, there is no proven link between higher gas limits and higher fees.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Is Hyperliquid’s [HYPE] current breakdown a potential bear trap before a $100 breakout?

Notably, analysts are not convinced. According to one analyst, Hyperliquid is breaking out of its rectangular consolidation pattern over the last 2 months. The key level to watch here is $75. On-chain activity is also providing a bullish signal. According to On-chain Lens, in the past 24 hours, Hyperliquid has burned $1.06 million of HYPE. This is a buyback process where HYPE is purchased using revenue from the protocol. The HYPE is then destroyed and removed completely from circulation.

More importantly, the pace of buybacks could increase if protocol revenue continues to rise.

As shown on the chart, Hyperliquid’s revenue has risen by 20% in under 24 hours, adding nearly $290k. Higher revenue could translate into larger buybacks, which can increase the amount of HYPE being removed from supply. This can provide further support for a recovery if the altcoin begins to see a rise in demand.

This is where HYPE’s historical price action and Binance’s withdrawal reopening become important. The continued buybacks and possible shortages mean a drop down to $53 is too much of a stretch. Instead, the breakdown could result in a bear trap, which catches late shorts and creates a July-style rally. Thus, a move toward $100 could still be on the cards for November.

#5209. Bitcoin CPI Reaction: 10 of 13 Cool Inflation Reports Triggered an Hourly Gain – Can Inflation Data Reverse the Downtrend?

Bitcoin CPI reaction: October CPI lands at 8:30 a.m. ET, core seen at 0.22%. Cool prints lifted BTC in the first hour 10 times out of 13.

The U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics publishes October’s consumer price index (CPI) on Tuesday, November 10, at 8:30 a.m. in New York, or 13:30 UTC. The Bitcoin CPI reaction that follows could set the tone for the week. The CPI is the main U.S. measure of inflation, and it will shape the Federal Reserve’s next rate decision.

Economists expect core prices, which leave out food and energy, to have risen about 0.22% in the month. That would be a slowdown from September’s 0.33%.

Economists expect growth in the core CPI to have moderated in October to around 0.22%, though the full range of estimates is broad enough to round down to 0.1% or round up to 0.3%.

Due to the government shutdown in Oct 2025, there's no tidy year-earlier figure. pic.twitter.com/CFM7ikw8fO

— Nick Timiraos (@NickTimiraos) November 9, 2026

Bitcoin trades near $74,400, still close to Sunday’s two-month low of $73,510.

CPI days move crypto. We checked Bitcoin’s first hour after the last 24 releases. When core inflation came in at 0.2% or lower, BTC rose in that hour 10 times out of 13. When it came in at 0.3% or higher, it rose only 4 times out of 10.

So which way will today’s number push Bitcoin?

The forecasts point to a calmer month. Seventeen banks and research firms surveyed by The Wall Street Journal see a median rise of 0.25% for headline CPI and 0.22% for core. Journal reporter Nick Timiraos posted the table on X.

That spread matters. The government reports CPI rounded to one decimal place. With forecasts this spread out, the core reading could print anywhere from 0.1% to 0.3%. A 0.3% would match September’s pace and read as inflation that is not cooling.

The yearly comparison will also be messy. The government shutdown in October 2025 meant no CPI was published for that month, so there is no clean year-over-year figure.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

This is the last CPI report the Federal Reserve will see before its December 8-9 meeting. Traders already expect a rate hike: on Tuesday morning, Polymarket gives an 86.5% chance of a 0.25-point increase and 11.5% for no change.

Higher rates tend to weigh on Bitcoin. They make cash and bonds pay more, which pulls money away from assets that pay no interest, like crypto.

A cool reading could shave those odds and give Bitcoin room to bounce. A hot one would lock in the hike.

DISCOVER: Fed Meeting Today: Traders Price a 95% Hold and a 79% December Hike. What Does It Mean for Crypto?

We measured the Bitcoin CPI reaction on Binance. We took the 24 CPI reports published since October 2024 and measured Bitcoin’s move in the hour after each 8:30 a.m. release.

The pattern is a lean, not a rule. The biggest drop came on February 12, 2025, when core rose 0.4% and BTC lost 1.9% in an hour. Last month’s 0.3% reading cost it 1.4%.

So far it has. BTC trades at $74,398 on Binance, about 1.2% above Sunday’s low.

On the daily chart, Bitcoin sits below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that weight recent prices more. The 200-day EMA is at $76,481 and the 50-day EMA at $79,128.

For anyone tracking the Bitcoin CPI reaction, the first hour after 8:30 a.m. is the one to watch. In 20 of the last 24 reports, Bitcoin ended the next 24 hours moving in the same direction as in that first hour.

#5208. Cardano Hits Epoch 666: Why Is It So Significant?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano (ADA) has entered epoch 666, yet another milestone for the blockchain, which has been live since its launch in September 2017.

The number has drawn attention for its connection to the biblical 666, but the development also points to the Cardano network's basic timekeeping and staking mechanism.

Popular Cardano blockchain explorer Cexplorer displays that epoch 666 started on Nov. 5, 2026, and is expected to end on Nov. 10, with 1 day and 10 hours left to close. The milestone marks another five-day cycle finished in Cardano's long-running proof-of-stake system.

Cardano divides its operation into epochs, which are five days long and consist of 432,000 one-second slots. The importance of epoch 666 is largely symbolic and not technical, as it does not coincide with any major protocol upgrades or hard forks. But the number 666 has merely provided an interesting talking point for the Cardano community.

In a separate milestone, the Cardano network has processed over 127,000,000 transactions on mainnet. According to Cexplorer data, Cardano has recorded a total of 127,203,164 transactions.

In upgrade news, DijkstraNet, the Dijkstra hard fork testnet, has been hard forked to protocol version 12 and is now available for testing and integration against the full Dijkstra feature set, with the exception of Linear Leios.

Node 11.2 was pre-released on October 28 and successfully used to transition DijkstraNet into the Dijkstra era, and DB Sync 13.8.0.0 was released for all networks.

In separate upgrade news, Midnight has completed its upgrade from Ledger 8 to Ledger 9, bringing new capabilities for developers alongside security, compatibility, and performance improvements across the network.

Ledger 9 introduces native support for widely used cryptographic standards including ECDSA and Keccak-256, expands wallet and custody compatibility, and enables contracts to interact directly with one another.

Keccak-256 is the standard algorithm that Ethereum uses to scramble data into fixed-length cryptographic fingerprints. ECDSA is the standard digital signature method that networks like Bitcoin and Ethereum use to prove ownership of a specific wallet address.

Speaking to his followers on X, the well-known economic and capital markets analyst warned of a potential Bitcoin plunge.

His remarks suggested that BTC is set to lose resilience due to recent events that have eroded investor confidence and pushed crypto prices lower.

Based on his market observation, the analyst noted that since the recently concluded 2026 midterm elections, U.S. stock markets have surged around 12% while Bitcoin dropped by 4.6%. Over the same period, gold and silver prices have risen by 5% and 7%, respectively.

According to Peter Schiff, price hikes in equities, gold, and silver indicate that Wall Street and traditional investors are celebrating the election outcome, marked by Democrats winning control of both the House and Senate.

Initially, investors feared a lengthy political battle after the Democratic sweep. However, traditional capital assets rebounded after Trump accepted the outcome, dispelling fears of an election dispute. In short, investors expect a divided Congress to clamp down on government spending and borrowing.

Also, the yield on the benchmark 10-year Treasury note dropped by 4.91% following the Democratic victory. This means lower Treasury yields support stock valuations by reducing borrowing costs and eroding bond returns, making them less attractive relative to equities and precious metals. This explains strong gains in U.S. stocks and rare metals.

Bitcoin weakened after the midterm election, noting that the timing indicates that the vote and the subsequent Binance hack impacted investor sentiment. Today, BTC trades at $73,135, after losing a crucial support at $75,000 and falling from its recent $80,000 peak.

Schiff is aware that Bitcoin always falls after midterm elections. He knows more drops are coming now that the asset has lost its key support at $75k. He believes that the cryptocurrency is primed to crash towards its cycle bottom (around $59,517), urging investors to sell before the short window closes.

To put it simply, Avalanche is where the receipts will get written.

According to a press release, Avalanche will be the core ledger for the clearing firm behind an upcoming NYSE-affiliated trading platform. Every trade will get cleared and settled on it.

The stocks themselves can be issued on other blockchains.

NYSE’s own Pillar engine will still handle the trading. The plan is round-the-clock trading, instant settlement and funding with stablecoins, though it’s pending regulator approval.

Why Avalanche though? According to Dennis Lee, the man who heads digital issuance at the NYSE, the exchange wanted a ledger that settles fast, keeps costs predictable, and lets them decide who gets in.

The timing here is interesting because this NYSE news is coming along the back of Avalanche’s best Q3 record.

On top of all that, the mainnet has handled 1.6 billion transactions in total!

In fact, its price shot up from $8.10 to $8.45, and it didn’t stop. AVAX tagged roughly $8.93 before settling near $8.83. That hinted at a 12.7% hike above the altcoin’s daily low.

Its RSI shot past 90 during the spike, before falling to 62.5 at press time. That was just below the Signal Line.

Finally, the DMI seemed to be bit more convincing. The ADX was at 56.7, hinting at a strong trend. Buyers seemed to be leading the charge, with +DI at 29.7 against -DI at 13.1.

If AVAX can push through $9, things are likely to get interesting.

#5204. Ether ETFs Shed $184.6M in a Day as BlackRock Leads Redemptions

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

The latest numbers from US spot Ether exchange-traded funds are another reminder that crypto’s institutional wrappers do not always move in sync with the networks beneath them. Farside Investors reports $184.6 million of net withdrawals on Monday, November 9. BlackRock’s ETHA accounted for $119.3 million, making it the largest identifiable contributor to the day’s negative reading.

TL;DR: US spot Ether ETFs lost $184.6 million on November 9. BlackRock’s ETHA recorded $119.3 million in outflows, with additional withdrawals from several other products. The latest session extends a run of weakness, but ETF flows alone cannot tell investors whether Ethereum’s network use or long-term adoption is improving.

Monday’s fund table is more revealing than a single headline number. Besides ETHA, the negative entries included Grayscale’s ETH product at $25 million, BlackRock’s ETHB at $20.8 million, Fidelity’s FETH at $19.1 million and TETH at $2 million. Morgan Stanley’s MSSE reported a smaller $1.6 million positive flow. Netting these issuer-level movements produces Farside’s total of $184.6 million leaving the category.

It was the third consecutive negative session, after withdrawals of $99.9 million on November 5 and $93.3 million on November 6. Across those dates, approximately $377.8 million left the tracked funds on a net basis. NewsBTC’s earlier look at the November 5 outflow provides a useful baseline, while our October divergence report shows how quickly relative Bitcoin and Ether fund demand can change.

An ETF owns or obtains exposure to ETH for its shareholders. It does not capture every place ETH is held, used as collateral, staked or exchanged. The Ethereum network also supports stablecoin transfers, decentralized finance applications, tokenized securities and settlement activity. Those services can grow or contract independently of whether a US investment wrapper receives subscriptions on a particular day.

Equally, network activity is not automatically a bullish signal for ETH’s market price. Fees, token issuance, layer-two activity and competition for users influence how economic use may translate into demand for the asset. Sensible analysis keeps both sets of evidence in view rather than choosing whichever currently supports a favored market narrative.

Bitcoin funds also recorded negative flows on November 9. That indicates this is not simply a one-day rotation from ETH into BTC within the US spot ETF category. Still, the products differ in investor base, structure, age and exposure, so the two daily totals should not be converted into a precise conclusion about relative institutional conviction.

One additional complication is reporting timing. Daily ETF tables are finalized after the US market closes and may be revised when an issuer reports late. Quoting an unfinished session as a completed result could create a false impression of reversal or acceleration. The figures here are dated specifically to November 9, and that time stamp should remain with the headline wherever the article is shared.

The next completed sessions will show whether ETHA’s withdrawals persist and whether other products begin drawing offsetting demand. Until then, Monday’s figures are strong evidence of weak investment-fund flows—not a definitive reading of the wider Ethereum economy or a forecast of the token’s next move.

#5203. XRP Price Plunges to $1.05 After a Hot CPI Report: Will the Support Stand?

Ripple XRP fell to $1.05 after a hot CPI report. After 7 of the last 10 hot reports, XRP was higher a day later. Can $1.05 hold?

Ripple XRP fell to $1.05 on Tuesday within five minutes of the U.S. October consumer price index (CPI) report, which showed XRP USD price rising faster than economists expected.

XRP crypto traded at $1.0797 when the report came out at 8:30 a.m. in New York, or 13:30 UTC. By 13:35 UTC it had touched $1.0500 on Binance, a 2.8% drop and its lowest price since August 19. It has since bounced to about $1.07. Bitcoin fell with it, dropping to $73,525 in the same five minutes.

The drop puts XRP back on a level it has tested before. Between late June and mid-August, XRP traded around $1.05 for weeks. The level held the first time and broke the second.

So will the support stand this time, and what do the CPI numbers tell us?

BREAKING: October CPI inflation rises +0.4% month-over-month, above expectations of +0.3%.

Core CPI inflation rose +0.3%, also above expectations of +0.2%.

BLS will not be releasing year-over-year figures due to the October 2025 government shutdown.

— The Kobeissi Letter (@KobeissiLetter) November 10, 2026

Prices rose faster than forecast in October. The Bureau of Labor Statistics said consumer prices rose 0.4% in the month, against the 0.3% economists expected.

Core prices, which leave out food and energy because they swing the most, rose 0.3%. The forecast was 0.2%. Core inflation has now run at about 0.3% for three months in a row.

The report is missing one usual figure. The bureau did not publish a yearly inflation rate because it never collected prices in October 2025, during the government shutdown. That leaves the monthly numbers to carry the whole report.

Those monthly numbers matter for the Federal Reserve. Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in a speech this morning that he already leans toward a 0.25 percentage point rate hike in December. If core CPI came in around 0.3% again, he said, a 0.5 point hike “should be considered”.

Core CPI did come in at 0.3%. Before the release, traders saw almost no chance of a hike that large. Higher rates make cash and bonds pay more, which tends to pull money out of risky assets like XRP.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Next Crypto to Hit 1 Dollar

The first move after a hot report has often faded. We measured XRP’s price on Binance in the 24 hours after each of the last 24 CPI reports, from October 2024 to October 2026.

Ten of those reports had core inflation of 0.3% or higher, like today’s. After seven of them, XRP was higher 24 hours later. The median move was a 1.4% gain.

The record is weaker this year. Of the four hot reports in 2026, XRP was higher a day later only twice. After the last one, on October 14, it fell 4.4% in 24 hours.

The difference this time is the Fed. A hot report that only confirms a small hike is easier for traders to shrug off. One that puts a larger hike on the table, as Waller laid out, gives sellers more to work with.

EXPLORE: XRP Price Forecast: Ripple at Risk of Losing Key Support, $1 Next?

The level has a history this year. XRP has spent two long stretches around $1.05 since June.

Today’s low of $1.0500 sits right on that zone. The first test of a level often holds. Each new test tends to leave fewer buyers there.

XRP trades at about $1.07 on Binance after the CPI drop, down 2.8% in 24 hours and 17% in a week. It is 71% below its July 2025 record of $3.65.

On the daily chart, where each candle covers one day, XRP sits well below both of its exponential moving averages (EMAs), averages that give recent prices more weight. The 50-day EMA stands at $1.294 and the 200-day EMA at $1.350.

For Ripple XRP, the first test of $1.05 has held so far. The daily close decides it: a close above $1.05 keeps the support alive, while a close below it opens the way to $1.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Top Solana Meme Coins to Bid

Telegram CEO Pavel Durov has shared new metrics on the Gram wallet integration in the messenger.

According to Durov, over the last 48 hours alone, users activated more than 700,000 non-custodial Gram wallets.

The screenshot shared by the Telegram CEO shows more than 329,000 new addresses a day. According to GRAM documentation, a wallet is created automatically when a user receives their first token transfer.

700K+ self-custodial wallets created on Telegram in the last 48 hours alone. Adoption spreads virally: a wallet activates the moment someone receives Grams. The crypto revolution is going strong. https://t.co/JPO2KVMip0

The growth comes after a series of changes Telegram made to the TON network earlier this year under Durov's "Make TON Great Again" campaign.

Back in 2020, Durov was forced to kill the original Telegram Open Network project and return more than $1.2 billion to investors after a lawsuit from the US SEC.

Community developers kept the open-source code alive as The Open Network (Toncoin).

This year, Telegram became the largest validator of the network. Besides, Telegram cut TON fees sixfold.

In June, the token was renamed back to its original name, Gram, after a community vote, with 81% of voters in favor. In July, Durov announced that Telegram would integrate a native non-custodial Gram wallet into every Telegram app.

The Telegram CEO described it as "the largest deployment of a non-custodial crypto wallet in history" and promised instant zero-fee transactions for more than 1 billion Telegram users.

According to TON developer Andrew Rogozov, the custodial Wallet in Telegram will stay separate from the new wallet. Now, onboarding happens natively within standard chat windows.

Still, while wallet creation metrics break records, liquidity inside the network is lagging behind with total value locked at $42.01 million, according to DefiLlama.

These hundreds of thousands of new addresses are mostly making microtransactions, such as buying Telegram Premium, paying for in-app ads, or settling small P2P transfers.

Besides, Telegram's validator weight is raising centralization concerns among industry critics. At the same time, the native wallet is still unavailable for US users.

At press time, Gram is trading at $1.05. The token is still more than 87% below its 2024 high of $8.25.

"The crypto revolution is going strong," Durov said.

#5200. MARA Sells 13,597 Bitcoin for $1.1B as Its AI Pivot Reshapes Treasury Strategy

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

MARA has put a sizeable number on the changing economics of Bitcoin mining. In its third-quarter shareholder letter, the company said it sold 13,597 BTC after September 30 for approximately $1.1 billion, at an average selling price of about $81,039. It used $750 million of the proceeds to repay Bitcoin-collateralized loans, releasing 17,477 BTC that had been pledged as security.

TL;DR: MARA disclosed $1.1 billion of post-quarter Bitcoin sales and repayment of $750 million in BTC-backed borrowing. The miner is increasingly monetizing its coin reserves to support AI infrastructure and other capital priorities. This marks a meaningful departure from its earlier strategy of holding mined Bitcoin.

At the end of September, MARA reported 34,012 BTC in total holdings, including a sizeable portion lent out or pledged as collateral. The newly disclosed disposals followed quarter-end, so that September balance should not be presented as the company’s current holding without adjustment. MARA said the repayments reduced the proportion of Bitcoin lent or pledged to less than 5% of its remaining holdings, versus roughly 57% at the earlier reporting date.

Management describes the move as a way to gain financial flexibility while still using Bitcoin strategically. Borrowing against the coins had helped finance the proposed acquisition of the Long Ridge natural-gas power business. The subsequent sale allowed MARA to clear the borrowing before the transaction closed, instead of leaving its financing exposed to future collateral calls if Bitcoin weakened.

That is a trade-off, not a free improvement. Selling reserve assets reduces direct exposure to any subsequent Bitcoin recovery. Keeping more BTC would preserve that upside but could require accepting additional liquidity and borrowing risk. The appropriate decision depends on the company’s priorities and its ability to earn a return on the infrastructure being funded.

MARA’s third-quarter revenue fell about 35% from a year earlier to $164.2 million. Its mining operations still produce coins, but the company is now emphasizing power assets and potential data-center customers alongside hashrate. Long Ridge includes generating capacity and land that could support a data-center campus. Development plans are not the same as signed, revenue-producing leases.

The move also fits a wider sector pattern. NewsBTC recently reported how Riot sold more than 20,000 BTC while pursuing AI-related data-center agreements. The contrast with Strive’s rapid Bitcoin accumulation is useful: public companies can use the same underlying asset in radically different corporate strategies.

MARA also disclosed authorization for up to $500 million in share repurchases, while its reported quarterly profit included substantial accounting effects linked to digital assets. Those items should not be mistaken for recurring mining cash flow. Nor does an authorization guarantee that every dollar of the program will be spent.

The follow-up questions are whether Long Ridge closes, whether planned data-center leases are signed, how much Bitcoin remains unrestricted, and whether new AI revenue eventually justifies the capital committed. The BTC sale is confirmed. The return on the assets it may finance is still an execution story.

#5199. Ripple Takes the Upper Hand Against Ethereum's Ethena in Stablecoin Race

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ripple USD (RLUSD) now tops the chart for short-term volume growth on decentralized exchanges (DEXs), outpacing Ethena's USDe. RWA Foundation, citing Token Terminal data, shows RLUSD's DEX trading volume jumped by $127.4 million over the past seven days.

USDe, the synthetic dollar issued by Ethena, has a bigger market cap of $4.97 billion at press time. Its DEX volume grew by $71.4 million, giving RLUSD a $56 million lead for the week.

Other major DeFi stablecoins, Usual's USD0 and Aave's GHO among them, stayed below $20 million.

Ethena offers roughly 5% yield through sUSDe staking. According to Ethena's documentation, the main risk of its delta-neutral model, which relies on short positions in perpetual futures, is prolonged negative funding rates.

USDe supply peaked above $14 billion in 2025. Ethena's fee switch and ENA buybacks are set to start only once the 14-day average USDe supply reaches $7.5 billion.

RLUSD is issued under the supervision of the New York State Department of Financial Services (NYDFS) and backed 100% by cash and short-term U.S. Treasuries, with monthly attestations of its reserves.

BNY acts as the custodian of RLUSD reserves.

In late October, the US Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) gave final approval to Ripple National Trust Bank. The bank will manage RLUSD reserves under federal oversight.

Besides it, RLUSD is available in Spark's Stablecoin FX pool on Uniswap v4, which lets corporate players make multi-million-dollar swaps.

According to RWA Foundation, about 92% of RLUSD turnover on the XRP Ledger (XRPL) runs through direct pairs with XRP.

Ripple also expanded its partnership with Robinhood. According to the companies, Bitstamp will use RLUSD on the XRP Ledger for settlement, while Robinhood plans to offer RLUSD to its retail customers by the end of the year.

At the same time, Coinbase added RLUSD to Coinbase Prime, Coinbase Exchange and its retail app, with 1:1 USD conversion on the XRP Ledger, Ethereum and Base.

At press time, RLUSD's market cap stands at $2.69 billion, roughly half USDe's size. The stablecoin started 2026 at about $1.28 billion.

#5198. Bitcoin – How BTC could turn its recent price crash into an $87K rally

Is Bitcoin’s [BTC] growing negative correlation to other risk assets becoming a key headwind?

It has been less than 48 hours since the most recent crypto wipeout resulted in over $1 billion in liquidations, pushing Bitcoin close to $70k, and sending market sentiment into extreme fear. While Bitcoin continues to trade below $75k, the S&P500 has risen by over 2%, pointing to stronger resilience despite the ongoing FUD.

This growing disparity raises questions about Bitcoin’s performance as a macro hedge this cycle. Notably, Peter Schiff shares his view. As his post below highlights, the long-time Bitcoin critic has warned of a deeper BTC correction, citing its underperformance compared to other risk assets. Even precious metals such as gold and silver have gained over 2%, further widening the performance gap.

Against this backdrop, positioning for further downside is starting to make more sense.

According to CoinGlass data, Bitcoin’s long/short ratio is showing bearish signs as it fell back from a 3-month high of 1.4 on the 7th of November, indicating traders are returning to shorting their positions, signaling weaker confidence in BTC’s near-term recovery. The key takeaway? There is no downside liquidity left to be hunted. The recent $1 billion liquidation event has wiped out almost all long-side liquidity.

This can be considered a great chance for bulls to recover. If Bitcoin remains above $75k and accumulation continues, then both early and late short positions could come under pressure. In fact, one analyst highlighted over $11 billion in short liquidity clustered around $87k.

Interestingly, bulls already seem to be positioning for this potential move.

Despite the scale of the recent wipeout, market sentiment is already showing signs of recovery.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index shows that sentiment has improved by four points to 32. This indicates that selling pressure may be losing steam and that confidence is returning. Notably, institutional activity is also adding to this shift. As on-chain data reported, MSTR recently bought an additional 1,036 BTC.

To add to this, MARA Holdings has announced a Q3 net profit of $634.4 million, up from $123.1 million in the previous year. This is backed up by Santiment data showing a distinct increase in accumulation between retail and mid-sized holders. In the past two weeks the market has seen an increase of 1-10 BTC wallets by 10,076 BTC and 10-100 BTC wallets by 4,131 BTC.

In short, just because BTC has failed to outperform other assets does not mean that the rally is dying.

Despite macro uncertainty and bearish positioning being a key factor, there are clear signs of increasing institutional exposure and accumulation across wallet classes. If Bitcoin can hold above $75k, the current divergence could turn into a potential bear trap, with the $87k short-liquidity cluster becoming the next major upside target.

Bitcoin is lagging traditional markets, but institutional buying and whale accumulation remain strong.

If Bitcoin holds $75k, a short squeeze could push prices toward $87k.

#5197. Strategy Adds 1,036 BTC While Repurchasing $188M of STRC Preferred Shares

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Strategy’s newest capital-allocation update contains two transactions that point in different directions. In a November 9 filing announcement, the company said it acquired 1,036 additional Bitcoin and bought back approximately $188 million of STRC preferred shares. The first adds to exposure to BTC; the second reduces outstanding securities in its wider financing structure.

TL;DR: Strategy has disclosed a 1,036-BTC purchase alongside $188 million of STRC repurchases. The combination shows that the company’s Bitcoin strategy involves ongoing capital management, not simply accumulation. These are separate transactions with different effects on liquidity, risk and future obligations.

Strategy is closely associated with long-term Bitcoin accumulation, and for good reason: its treasury is far larger than that of most public companies. Yet the recurring filings increasingly include details about preferred securities, reserves and capital raising that are central to understanding how that position is financed.

A Bitcoin purchase turns available funds into an asset whose market value can change around the clock. Repurchasing preferred shares does something else. It reduces the quantity of a security issued to outside investors and may alter future distribution obligations, depending on that security’s terms. One action raises direct BTC exposure; the other changes the claims that investors have on Strategy’s capital structure.

That distinction was visible in an earlier update covered by NewsBTC, when Strategy bought 811 BTC and repurchased $208.2 million of STRC. The new announcement continues that paired approach, rather than revealing a sudden switch away from its central investment thesis.

Preferred securities can offer investors contractual distributions or other rights that differ from ordinary equity. Their terms, market prices and the company’s reserve position matter for both existing holders and common shareholders. Buying them back may be attractive when they trade at prices the issuer considers advantageous, but it also uses liquidity that could otherwise remain in reserve or fund more purchases.

There is a useful comparison with other Bitcoin treasury companies, which have their own funding methods and varying tolerance for leverage. The phrase ‘corporate Bitcoin buyer’ hides important differences in debt maturities, preferred distributions, equity issuance and the sources of cash used to acquire coins.

Strategy’s public metrics indicate Bitcoin holdings around 850,364 BTC following the update. Readers should use the company’s dated disclosure when comparing holdings and purchase prices rather than combining a fresh transaction with an older snapshot. Market-value figures can change between the filing and the moment a reader opens the article.

Another consideration is the difference between a repurchase authorization and completed repurchases. Here the company is describing actual STRC repurchase activity, which should be counted separately from any standing authority to buy securities later. The price paid and reduction in outstanding shares determine its economic impact; simply repeating the dollar amount cannot answer those questions.

None of this proves that the company’s securities or Bitcoin will rise. A more useful test is whether Strategy can keep servicing its obligations while making purchases in a period of changing interest rates and volatile digital-asset prices. The November 9 announcement confirms it is still doing both jobs—adding coins and adjusting the financing architecture that supports the strategy.

"But you're running out of cash. And the selling pressure on STRC will increase as Bitcoin crashes," Schiff wrote.

"It won't be nearly enough yield to cover the risk. 12%," he warned.

The stern comments from Schiff followed a typical bullish tweet from Saylor. In his post, Saylor claimed that AI could not mint new Bitcoin.

"AI can make me rap. It can't print Bitcoin," the cofounder of Strategy wrote.

Strategy's Variable Rate Series A Perpetual Stretch Preferred Stock (STRC) seeks to provide regular dividend income to investors while trading near its $100 face value.

Currently, STRC offers a 12% annual dividend yield, which Strategy has been working to keep steady in order to keep the stock trading near its face value.

Describing the heavy buybacks as a success, the firm said it had successfully put STRC on the road to financial recovery.

#5193. Visa Adds Monad to Stablecoin Settlement Options as Institutions Seek More Rails

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Visa’s growing list of blockchain settlement options reportedly has a new entrant: Monad. According to a November 10 report citing the Monad Foundation, participating Visa issuers and acquirers can now use the network for stablecoin settlement. That is a back-office financial infrastructure development, rather than a new consumer-facing Visa card that automatically makes every merchant accept cryptocurrency.

When a cardholder pays at a shop, the retail transaction and the subsequent movement of funds between financial institutions are separate processes. A settlement service can use digital dollars on a blockchain to transfer value between participating institutions without requiring consumers to interact with a crypto wallet. That is the layer Visa has been expanding.

Visa’s October 15 update on stablecoin-powered money movement showed that parts of its broader digital-dollar strategy were moving from pilots toward live commercial deployment. Monad’s reported addition extends the selection of networks available to eligible partners; it should not be confused with a universal launch in every market or for every Visa transaction.

For an issuer or acquirer, more than one usable settlement rail can provide choices around transaction processing, cost, operational resilience and supported assets. But multiple chains also bring added technical and compliance responsibilities. Connecting a network is the beginning of integration, not proof that it will process meaningful volumes immediately.

NewsBTC previously examined how Visa’s treasury engine targets institutional settlement. Separately, Reap’s Monad-linked card infrastructure plan illustrates a different use of the same chain: backing consumer card activity, not necessarily settling balances between institutions.

A blockchain being listed among supported options can improve its credibility with enterprise developers, but that does not establish a direct relationship between network adoption and the market price of its native token. Fees, throughput, partner concentration and the way transaction costs are paid all influence the economics.

What matters next is confirmation of which issuers and acquirers are live, which stablecoins and regions are supported, how settlement is reconciled when something fails, and what measurable transaction volume arrives. Those details would turn a promising infrastructure announcement into evidence of commercial activity. Until then, this is a meaningful new option—not an automatic overhaul of how Visa payments work everywhere.
