#5192. North Korean Hackers Flip Stolen Binance Funds Into NEAR Meme Coins

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

North Korea's TraderTraitor group has started laundering funds stolen from Binance through newly created meme tokens, according to Elliptic.

The $6.4 billion Binance hack took place in October 2026 and was attributed to TraderTraitor.

According to Elliptic, the hackers have used this technique on Tron and NEAR, while a similar approach has appeared on Solana.

Between Nov. 5 and 7, 2026, the hackers launched at least six of these tokens on NEAR: FUK, OLCCL, MEILI, MMLI, DABI and SINU. Launching one cost around 25 NEAR (about $90).

According to Elliptic, the hackers paired each token with NEAR in a liquidity pool on a decentralized exchange (DEX). Wallets holding stolen NEAR deposited it into the pool and received the new token. A second set of wallets then sold the token back into the pool and withdrew NEAR.

This way, the funds never move directly from the wallets holding stolen assets to the receiving wallets.

According to Elliptic, the receiving wallets look like ordinary traders who sold a token at a profit.

Each token got typical meme branding, from monkeys to an image of Elon Musk under an "Elon 2028 President" banner. Elliptic noted that Musk is not linked to the tokens.

Each token was sold for the first time less than three hours after launch, and at least 75% of its trading took place within one day. None of the six tokens has traded since Nov. 8, and the creators of four of them withdrew the remaining liquidity on that day.

In total, 117,000 NEAR (about $421,000) moved through the scheme. Most of it then went into stablecoins or to a cross-chain bridge.

Elliptic noted that five of the six token creators received funds from a wallet that had been selling the previous token 2 to 13 minutes before the new launch, and in this way the operation funded itself.

The only outside activity was a few small purchases of FUK on its first day, which accounted for less than 1% of its volume, Elliptic said.

According to Elliptic, the on-chain trail stays public, and the technique makes tracing the funds slower. The hackers can also create many tokens and abandon each one after use.

In 2025, after the Bybit hack, the same scheme moved at least $24 million through Tron. In that case, the hackers paired a worthless token with a stablecoin.

In the Binance case, Elliptic identified a similar token on Tron on Nov. 2. On Nov. 3, stolen SOL was swapped into existing thinly traded tokens on Solana and then sold for USDC.

Elliptic said that so far only a small part of the stolen funds has gone through meme tokens. About 11.8 million NEAR from the theft has not moved yet, and at press time, NEAR is trading at $3.60, which puts these funds at around $42.4 million.

Elliptic also advises compliance teams to check funds coming from liquidity pools, especially pools with newly created tokens.

"Laundering is a race to move funds before they are flagged," Elliptic added.

#5190. Standard Chartered Expands Prime Brokerage Partnership With 24X to Cover BTC and ETH

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Institutional investors will be able to access Bitcoin and Ether trading through an expanded relationship between 24X and Standard Chartered. In a November 10 announcement, 24X Bermuda said the banking group will extend its prime-brokerage role from foreign-exchange products to digital assets on 24X’s multi-asset technology platform.

TL;DR: 24X says Standard Chartered is expanding its prime-brokerage relationship to cover BTC and ETH. The arrangement is aimed at institutional counterparties accessing crypto and FX through one execution infrastructure. It changes the trading and counterparty workflow for eligible participants; it is not a retail exchange rollout.

The company’s stated model allows an institutional participant to face Standard Chartered as its central counterparty while using 24X for execution, matching, liquidity configuration and straight-through processing. That separation matters. The participant’s commercial relationship and credit exposure are handled differently from the technology that matches orders.

Traditional foreign exchange and spot cryptocurrency trading do not have identical market structures or risk profiles. FX markets have established bank relationships, while crypto markets operate around the clock and rely on a patchwork of exchanges, brokers and custody providers. An integrated platform can simplify some operational tasks, although it still requires clients to understand the product-specific terms.

The release says 24X already offered FX, non-deliverable forwards and spot services. BTC and ETH are the additional digital-asset instruments named in the expanded partnership. It would be misleading to claim the agreement immediately covers every token, derivative or jurisdiction.

A recognized bank acting as a central relationship may appeal to institutions that want more familiar onboarding, credit arrangements and oversight. It does not remove Bitcoin volatility, market liquidity problems or the possibility of disruptions in the surrounding infrastructure. Nor should the word ‘prime’ be used as a shortcut for saying all trades are insured or guaranteed.

Institutions will need to evaluate exposure limits, settlement timing, custody arrangements, collateral terms and what happens during market stress. The distinction between an execution venue and the organization responsible for a trading obligation is especially important for crypto, where the history of market failures has made counterparty concentration a persistent concern.

NewsBTC previously looked at Ripple Prime’s effort to bring traditional markets into 24-hour trading workflows. That is a distinct product and operator, but it reflects the same strategic demand: customers increasingly want to manage digital and conventional exposures without jumping between disconnected systems.

The announcement establishes the partnership expansion, but it does not provide a full ledger of live client trades, volumes or fee terms. Commercial success depends on whether eligible counterparties find the combined trading and brokerage model attractive enough to move business from existing venues.

As tokenized securities and digital assets enter more established markets, investors may become less focused on whether a blockchain is present and more concerned with the legal machinery behind settlement. Our report on the NYSE’s tokenized securities infrastructure partners describes why those arrangements matter. For 24X, the next proof point will be active institutional adoption rather than the breadth of the announcement alone.

#5188. Greece Passes Crypto Tax Law With 10% Gains Levy, 15% on Staking and DeFi

Greece Passes Crypto Tax Law With 10% Gains Levy, 15% on Staking and DeFi

Greece's parliament passed a bill Thursday imposing a 10% capital-gains tax on cryptocurrency profits, marking a shift from a proposal discussed in October to legislation approved by lawmakers.

The measure also puts a 15% tax on income from staking, crypto lending and decentralized finance, or DeFi.

The legislation’s crypto provisions sit inside a wider Finance Ministry package dealing with debt servicing, financial oversight, and government accounting, with the new crypto income-tax rules scheduled for January 1, 2027.

Individual investors with annual net crypto gains of €500 ($557) or less will be exempt. Cross that threshold, though, and the 10% tax applies to the entire gain, rather than just the amount over €500 ($557).

For example, an investor with €600 ($669) in net gains would face a €60 ($67) tax under the new rate, not €10 ($11). The rules generally calculate gains by subtracting the acquisition cost from the value received when the crypto is disposed of.

Selling Bitcoin for euros is one taxable event, but spending crypto on goods or services can also count as a disposal. Investors will need transaction records to establish what they paid for their assets and what those assets were worth when sold or spent.

Yield from staking, lending, and providing liquidity to DeFi protocols is treated differently. Greece will classify those returns as interest income and tax them at 15%, according to the Finance Ministry.

Qualifying crypto losses exceeding €500 ($557) can be carried forward for five years and offset against future crypto gains. The bill also gives investors a 12-month window to voluntarily declare previously unreported crypto income without late-filing penalties or interest, provided they meet its conditions and pay within 60 days of filing.

The passage follows an October public consultation on the proposal.The draft proposed a 10% rate that was already lower than the 15% levy Greek officials had discussed in June.

Athens says exchanging one cryptocurrency for another should not itself trigger a capital-gains tax. But the wording lawmakers approved may not make that exclusion as clear as the government intended.

A review by parliament's Scientific Service flagged the bill's broad language covering disposals made "for consideration." That phrase could include token-to-token trades, the review said, even though the accompanying explanation says such swaps should not create taxable gains.

The reviewers recommended an explicit exemption and suggested clearer rules for exchanges involving stablecoins and electronic-money tokens. Those details matter for traders who rotate between assets without cashing out to fiat currency.

Greece is also preparing the administrative rules needed to put the tax into practice. A senior Finance Ministry official told Reuters that guidance on transaction transparency and tax audits would be issued in the coming weeks.

The government has not estimated how much the tax will raise. Officials say the market is difficult to measure because many Greek crypto investors use trading platforms based overseas.

#5186. Charles Schwab Opens Live Solana, Avalanche and Chainlink Trading to Eligible Clients

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Charles Schwab has moved three major altcoins from its expansion plan into its live crypto trading menu. The brokerage announced on November 9 that eligible Schwab Crypto clients can now trade Solana, Avalanche and Chainlink, alongside Bitcoin and Ethereum. The distinction between ‘planned’ and ‘available’ is the new milestone here.

TL;DR: Schwab Crypto now supports SOL, AVAX and LINK trading for eligible retail clients, adding to its BTC and ETH offering. Schwab says its service became broadly available to qualifying customers in October 2026. The announcement concerns direct crypto trading, not approval of new exchange-traded funds for these tokens.

Schwab began offering early access to its crypto service in May and opened it to eligible retail clients in October. The November addition broadens a product already running in the market, rather than announcing a future concept with no users. That gives the three altcoins a route to customers who prefer established brokerage infrastructure and account management over a separate crypto-native trading platform.

NewsBTC covered the earlier Schwab expansion plan in August. The new press release supplies the operational update those readers would have been waiting for: the named assets are available, subject to Schwab’s account and jurisdiction requirements.

This is an important qualification because access is not universal. Customers must qualify for the service, and conventional brokerage protections or product terms should never be assumed to apply identically to every digital-asset holding. Investors should read the published risk disclosures and custody arrangement rather than relying on the familiarity of the corporate brand.

Solana is a smart-contract network whose economic activity includes trading, payments and tokenized assets. Avalanche operates a different blockchain ecosystem with an emphasis on customizable network infrastructure. Chainlink is associated with data and connectivity services used by smart contracts. They are all crypto assets, but they are not interchangeable bets on a single technology.

That distinction helps explain why broadening a brokerage menu does not immediately establish institutional conviction in any particular network. A broker chooses instruments for a combination of liquidity, client interest, operational readiness and risk controls. Adding a symbol is a meaningful access decision; it is not a research conclusion that the asset must outperform.

For further ecosystem context, NewsBTC’s recent look at Solana’s network upgrades and tokenized-asset activity shows why a network’s technical evolution should be assessed separately from the price of a token on a brokerage screen.

The launch raises useful questions: how many customers will open or fund Schwab Crypto accounts, how much activity will flow into the new assets, and whether more tokens follow? The announcement does not provide day-one trading volumes or say how much demand each token will receive.

A larger, more familiar distribution channel could change the market over time by widening who can participate. But whether it deepens liquidity or simply moves existing traders from one provider to another will require actual usage data. For now, the verified news is an operational launch with a clearly defined set of supported coins.

#5184. Treasury Cuts Crypto Exchange MEXC Off From US Financial System

Treasury Cuts Crypto Exchange MEXC Off From US Financial System

The U.S. Treasury Department has moved to sever crypto exchange MEXC Global from the American financial system, accusing it of facilitating at least $16 billion in illicit transactions tied to Iran, North Korea and Russia.

In an order issued Friday through its Financial Crimes Enforcement Network, Treasury said MEXC, one of the world’s 20 largest crypto exchanges, served as a major conduit for sanctions evasion and cybercrime.

The action, part of a broader effort dubbed Operation Economic Outcast, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing any funds transfers to or from the exchange. It takes effect 30 days after publication in the Federal Register.

“Financial institutions cannot have it both ways: Either you support the Iranian regime, or you have access to the U.S. financial system,” Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said in a statement, vowing to keep severing the regime’s “economic lifelines” and its attempts to use digital assets to dodge sanctions.

Founded in 2018, MEXC processes more than $1 billion in daily transactions, holds around $5 billion in reserves, and offers more than 3,000 tokens, according to FinCEN. Between May 2021 and May 2026, FinCEN said, the platform moved at least $16 billion connected to illicit activity, including nearly $1.5 billion tied to Russian sanctions evasion, with $232 million in direct transactions with sanctioned entities. The agency named MEXC a “primary money laundering concern” in connection with Russian illicit finance.

Despite publicly claiming anti-money-laundering controls and barring users in places like Iran and North Korea, MEXC’s compliance was riddled with “severe deficiencies,” FinCEN said.

The exchange was still transacting with sanctioned Russian exchange Garantex and its successor Grinex as recently as June 2026, more than four years after Garantex was first designated. It also processed roughly $200 million linked to the A7A5 stablecoin and about $22 million benefiting Iran’s central bank and its Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps.

Those connections place MEXC squarely within a sprawling sanctions-evasion ecosystem that Treasury has been dismantling piece by piece.

The department last month designated Russia’s A7 Network a transnational criminal organization and proposed cutting its front companies off from U.S. finance, flagging more than $179 billion in transactions through its ruble-backed A7A5 token, much of it routed through Garantex and Grinex.

The same campaign has reached into Iran’s crypto sector, where Treasury gained sweeping authority to sanction anyone operating in it and blacklisted the Iranian exchange Nobitex over its role in funneling crypto to the IRGC.

The order uses a provision of the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act rather than a standard sanctions designation, a tool FinCEN has increasingly reached for to cut off crypto infrastructure that operates outside the correspondent banking system.

#5183. Why Cardano’s 57% staked supply signals resilience despite ADA price dip

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Coinbase data showed that 57% of the Cardano [ADA] supply was staked. The estimated reward rate ranges from 1.30% to 3.00% APY, with Coinbase’s rate at 1.38%. The high-staked supply showed network resilience and strong conviction from holders.

In other news, Node 11.2 is live on the mainnet and has brought core upgrade features. AMBCrypto reported that node configs came in a new “envelope” format that nodes older than 11.2 would not be able to read, which means operators will have to upgrade.

Despite the news, the price reaction has been minimal so far.

The MVRV ratio measures how much profit or loss the average investor is sitting on. Santiment data also tracks MVRV data across different rolling timeframes, such as 30-day (short-term holder) and 180-day (long-term holder).

The data showed that both the 30-day and 180-day MVRV ratios were below zero. In other words, these holders were facing losses, on average, of 15.21% and 8.9%, respectively.

The September rally saw the MVRV ratios reach highly positive territory, especially for the 180-day cohort. It was expected that this would prompt profit-taking and selling pressure. The sustained ADA price losses below $0.20 were also due to crypto market-wide fear as Bitcoin faced rejection below $80k.

The daily active addresses numbers have tumbled compared to levels registered in the first half of October too, from a peak of 29k to just 12k-14k in recent days.

The price correction to $0.194 in mid-September saw the 2-year dormant circulation spike, and also caused a minor dip in the 2-year mean coin age.

These metrics measure the amount of tokens not transacted for a specific period of time, and the average number of days all ADA tokens were in their addresses, in a rolling window of the chosen time period (for example, 90 days).

Over the past six weeks, while Cardano was in steady decline, both the 90-day and 2-year MCA have trended higher. Meanwhile, the dormant circulation was relatively quiet.

This relative quiet was markedly different from the panicked offloading seen in June. Back then, Cardano prices were crashing towards the $0.14-$0.15 lows. Dormant circulation was high, and the mean coin age was in freefall.

Therefore, despite the steady drawdown in recent weeks, the sentiment was likely quite different, as the evidence at hand shows.

#5182. Walt Launches Standalone Crypto App After Years Inside Telegram

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

The wallet platform that grew up inside Telegram is trying to become a destination in its own right. Walt, previously branded Wallet in Telegram, launched a standalone Android app on November 10. The company says an iOS version will follow. For the first time, customers can sign up without using Telegram, with Google and Apple among the supported account routes.

TL;DR: Walt has launched a separate Android app while keeping its existing Telegram integration. The service advertises access to more than 200 cryptocurrencies, tokenized US stocks and ETFs, perpetual futures and yield products, subject to regional availability. iOS is planned, not already live as part of today’s announcement.

The new app largely bundles products Walt had already developed inside Telegram. That means the headline is not the invention of a new trading contract or token. The commercial difference is that Walt no longer needs a user to start in a messaging app before becoming a customer.

According to the company’s product announcement, the app offers more than 200 cryptocurrencies, over 100 tokenized US stocks and exchange-traded funds, and perpetual contracts linked to crypto and some traditional asset classes. Walt also advertises yield features and AI-assisted research and trading tools. Availability and restrictions differ by jurisdiction and by individual product.

The company’s rebranding guidance explains that Walt and Telegram’s separate Money wallet are different services. That matters for users who remember an older ‘Wallet’ label and could easily assume the products now share an operator or custody arrangement.

Walt says existing users can sign in through Telegram and access the same funds through the standalone app. New users can register without Telegram. This shared-account design may reduce the inconvenience of switching channels, but users should still confirm the account recovery, security and withdrawal rules of the actual wallet service they use.

There is also an important distinction between owning a tokenized representation of a US stock and holding the original share through a conventional brokerage account. Product terms determine shareholder rights, corporate actions and redemption. NewsBTC’s coverage of transfer agents in tokenized securities describes why those legal and operational details are central to a credible market.

NewsBTC has also examined Visa’s institutional stablecoin settlement expansion. That infrastructure serves a different audience, but it underlines how wallet apps and payment networks are both trying to make digital balances more usable outside speculative trading.

The new app’s perpetual futures are another product that deserves clear risk labeling. They provide leveraged or derivative exposure rather than ownership of the underlying asset, and can carry liquidation and funding risks even when presented in a convenient mobile interface.

Walt’s distribution advantage has been access to Telegram users, but downloads, funded accounts and repeated use are different measures. The company says it has a large registered audience without publishing a full current breakdown of active users and trade volumes in this announcement. Those are the numbers that would help measure the standalone app’s impact.

The next milestones are an iOS release, geographic availability, fees and the adoption of products users cannot obtain as conveniently in the Telegram interface. Walt has now delivered the separate app. Whether customers will make it their primary home for digital assets remains an open commercial question.

#5181. Ripple's Schwartz Debunks XRP Price Myth

David Schwartz, the former chief technology officer at Ripple and one of the creators of the XRP Ledger (XRPL), has dispelled one of the most common myths regarding the key factors that could impact the price of XRP.

The former Ripple CTO took to the X social media platform to challenge the idea that the use of XRP as the native gas token of the XRP Ledger could be a major factor in determining the price of the cryptocurrency.

"I don't think anybody who knows anything has said that the use of XRP as gas will be a major driver of the price of XRP," Schwartz wrote.

The comment was made as the Ripple veteran took part in the ever-growing debate centered around the poor price performance of XRP and the place of the cryptocurrency in Ripple's ever-expanding ecosystem.

The former Ripple CTO directed the audience's attention to a post from November 2025, in which he explained his thoughts on how mass adoption of the XRP Ledger could lead to greater value for the ledger's native cryptocurrency.

"The increased use and adoption of XRPL will add value to XRP by its privileged place on the ledger," Schwartz said.

He emphasized that XRP has several unique properties, including the lack of counterparty risk, the fact that it's jurisdictionless, and its privileged place in the network's autobridging features.

In addition, Schwartz argued that it would be hard to imagine the XRP Ledger succeeding on a mass scale without XRP capturing a significant portion of the value created by such success.

"I really do think it's hard to think we could end up in a world where XRPL is successful and XRP doesn't get a good fraction of that value," he said.

Nevertheless, Schwartz also said earlier that the XRP Ledger was not created with the sole intent of pushing the price of XRP higher. Instead, the original goal was to create a fast and cheap payment system with a native digital asset and a decentralized exchange feature.

Schwartz's recent comments come as frustration among XRP holders over the poor performance of the cryptocurrency is on the rise.

As of Monday, the Ripple-related token was trading at around the $1.09 level, down more than 12% in the last seven days.

Recently, Schwartz admitted that his influence on the market price of XRP is very small.

"Do I have anything to do with the price of XRP? Honestly? I guess probably not anything. But very little," he wrote during another interaction with critics.

#5180. Coachella 2027 Lineup Leaks: Polymarket Odds Reveal the Top 3 Favorites

Home » Crypto Market News » Coachella 2027 Lineup Leaks: Polymarket Odds Reveal the Top 3 Favorites

Industry leaks point to major pop and rock icons—here is who prediction markets expect to officially take the stage.

Rumors about the Coachella 2027 lineup picked up steam after reports and social media posts pointed to SZA, Dua Lipa, and Tame Impala as the festival’s possible headliners.

While the music festival’s promoter Goldenvoice has yet to confirm any performers, crypto-based prediction markets reacted instantly to the industry buzz.

On Polymarket, the leading Web3 prediction platform, capital flowed rapidly into event contracts as crypto traders rushed to price in the latest reporting.

Industry buzz began picking up momentum after reports from media outlets, including TMZ, indicated that talks were underway with Dua Lipa and Tame Impala.

The speculation spiked further when R&B star SZA seemingly corroborated the rumors online, pointing toward a headlining trio representing three distinct corners of modern pop, R&B, and psychedelic rock.

Reports also suggest that festival organizers had approached other massive acts—including BTS, Oasis, Radiohead, and Rage Against the Machine—before cementing focus on the current trio.

Crypto-based prediction markets have become a real-time barometer of public sentiment when entertainment leaks surface. The rumors have also played out on Polymarket, where odds shift instantly as new information emerges.

Polymarket’s latest odds show strong confidence in the leaked trio.

As of November 10, SZA has a 74% implied probability of appearing on the lineup. Dua Lipa follows at 65%, with $9,917 in trading volume—the highest individual total among all listed artists—marking a potential milestone for her first solo Coachella headlining performance. Beyond the top three, other artists are also drawing bets. BLACKPINK has a 45% implied probability, followed by Drake at 28%.

Other names further down the list include Travis Scott at 16%, Harry Styles at 15%, Daft Punk at 13%, BTS at 7%, and Justin Bieber at 5%.

Trading volume across Coachella-related markets climbed within hours of the rumors spreading on social media. The market is scheduled to remain open until January 31, 2027.

The reaction to the Coachella 2027 lineup is a prime example of how prediction markets react to mainstream entertainment news long before official announcements drop. Traders aggregate information from media reports, social media leaks, and industry chatter to price in probabilities.

While these odds do not represent guaranteed confirmation from the promoter Goldenvoice, they offer a direct view into market consensus—one that shifts dynamically as fresh details emerge.

One of the world’s biggest music festivals is no stranger to blockchain. In 2022, Coachella auctioned ten lifetime-pass NFTs. The collection raised roughly $1.4 million. After FTX’s collapse that November, many holders lost access to their keys, as assets kept on the exchange became stuck.

In 2024, the festival tried again with OpenSea on Avalanche, reissuing NFTs to affected buyers, though demand for the first new collection was initially weak.

#5179. Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

Gamers in low-income areas will be among the hardest hit by Sony’s decision to stop producing physical game discs from 2028, experts told Decrypt, while one African games industry expert arguing the continent stands to lose out badly.

The secondary market has long been a cheap way for gamers to get hold of games. The PlayStation Store does run sales, but buying digitally assumes a decent internet connection, which rural households are less likely to have.

"By abandoning physical discs, some players at the bottom of the economic spectrum lose access to physical distribution through retail and, more importantly, discounted used sales," Joost van Dreunen, professor at the NYU Stern School of Business and video game business author, told Decrypt. "It won't immediately show up in the financials of major game makers, since their focus is now on creating premium experiences for a luxury class of gamers."

It comes as the cost of being a gamer is trending upwards. Sony raised PS5 prices in March, for example, citing "pressures in the global economic landscape." Similarly, the next generation of Xbox is expected to cost up to $1,800, according to Fortune.

A spokesperson for UK advocacy group Gamer's Voice told Decrypt that Sony's move to cut discs will "likely affect people on lower incomes more."

Isa Muhammad, founder of Africa and Middle East-focused games industry platform Bijetic, told Decrypt that the PS5 is already "out of reach for most Africans." He stressed that those who can buy a console still rely on the second-hand games market to afford to play on the consoles.

Well, at least the highest-income Africans could still take advantage of the PlayStation Store discounts, right? Unfortunately not. Muhammad said that the PlayStation Network is only supported in one African nation: South Africa. Many other nations are missing from the PlayStation Network's official list of supported countries.

"A gamer in Nigeria who wants to buy digitally has to set up an account registered in the U.S. or UK, then pay with foreign gift cards or a dollar card," Muhammad said.

Download speeds in rural, remote, and low-income areas are another major hurdle.

"Bandwidth is now part of what a game costs," van Dreunen said. "Roughly one in four rural [American] households has no wired provider offering 100/20 Mbps, and at 10 Mbps a 50GB download takes about 11 hours."

In Africa and the Middle East, Muhammad said, internet speeds vary significantly across the region—from super-fast connections in Dubai to unreliable ones in Iran. Internet access is also costly in some countries.

"Just recently I paid 75,000 NGN (around $55) for a 450GB broadband bundle, which is more than Nigeria's minimum monthly wage," Muhammad told Decrypt. With GTA 6's pre-load file expected to be 212GB, "a gamer here would first spend $80 on the game, then anywhere from $25 to over $60 just to download it, depending on how they buy data."

And at Nigeria's median download speed of 36.77 Mbps, per Speedtest by Ookla, the download would take nearly 13 hours. Not to mention the fact that they'd need to have a non-Nigerian PlayStation account, a download code corresponding to the country their account is supposedly from, and a way to pay in the local currency.

"Sony's own terms say you must register your account in the country where you live, and Sony can suspend or close accounts created with false information," Muhammad told Decrypt. "So every Nigerian or Egyptian on a US or UK PSN account is breaking the rules from day one."

African gamers live in fear of needing help from Sony and being refused it (or worse, banned) because their accounts break its rules, Muhammad explained. In a future where every purchase is digital, it could open the door to gamers losing accounts that hold every game they've ever purchased.

"If a player in Africa buys GTA 6 and can't recover their account, they may have no choice but to buy it again on a new one, along with everything else they've lost," he said. "A disc-free future will see this become the reality for every new PlayStation game, for gamers across most of Africa."

Sony and PlayStation did not respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

#5178. Bitcoin price prediction: JPMorgan’s $150K bull case for 2030 vs. $84,000 resistance

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The crypto market feels cautious, with traders selling rallies instead of chasing them. Bitcoin sits at the center of that mood after a new forecast argued it may stay below $150,000 through 2030.

The call lands as Bitcoin trades far below its record high and just above a key support level. Any Bitcoin price prediction for the coming weeks starts with whether buyers can defend that floor.

Traders who weigh long-term forecasts against short-term swings also watch early-stage projects. One of them is MEMASA ($MEMASA), an upcoming AI trading app that lets users research and trade crypto through a chat.

A recent Motley Fool analysis on Yahoo Finance argues Bitcoin will likely stay below $150,000 through the end of the decade.

Author Dominic Basulto says the $1 million targets popular among Bitcoin bulls should be shelved for now.

His math leans on JPMorgan Asset Management, which expects Bitcoin to beat stocks, bonds and gold over the next decade.

The firm’s bull case assumes yearly growth near 17%, which would land Bitcoin close to that ceiling on the same timeline.

Still, JPMorgan warns the ride will come with extreme volatility, and Bitcoin has lost about 40% over the past year. That slump explains why even Strategy’s Michael Saylor has hinted that a return to six figures may prove difficult.

Bitcoin now trades near $74,800, down about 10% over the past month. The nearest strong support sits near $73,500, while resistance stands much higher at about $84,400. Technical indicators lean bearish, so a clean break below support could open a path toward $70,000.

For any near-term Bitcoin price prediction, reclaiming $84,400 would be the first sign that buyers are back in control. Long-term forecasts like JPMorgan’s leave traders with plenty of questions, and some new tools aim to answer them faster.

MEMASA is an upcoming AI crypto trading app that lets users analyze markets, check token risks, and trade on-chain by chatting with an AI assistant.

The app tackles problems familiar to crypto traders: too many charts, scattered market data, scam tokens, and complicated interfaces. MEMASA plans to combine market intelligence, risk checks, automated strategies, and trading in one self-custodial app.

If the crypto market enters another bullish phase, growing interest in altcoins could bring more people into trading.

MEMASA aims to serve that audience by making market research and trading as simple as a conversation. Its potential appeal extends from beginners struggling with trading interfaces to experienced traders who want faster access to market insights.

Can $MEMASA benefit from the next crypto rally?

$MEMASA is the utility token powering the MEMASA ecosystem. Its planned uses include premium AI intelligence, trading fee discounts, marketplace payments, and staking for analysts and AI agents.

The token’s potential derives from stronger sentiment across the crypto market and growing demand within the MEMASA ecosystem. If the app attracts a wide audience, it might drive the demand for $MEMASA.

MEMASA is currently offering early access to $MEMASA through an on-chain early sale, with prices below the project’s anticipated public listing price. Earlier stages offer lower prices, while later stages provide faster token unlocks.

A Bitcoin rally could create favorable conditions for MEMASA’s debut. The bigger test will be whether the app delivers on its promise to simplify crypto trading and attracts enough users to build lasting demand for its token.

Bitcoin’s next test is the support near $73,500. Holding it keeps a recovery toward $84,400 in play, while a break could send the price toward $70,000.

The long view looks calmer, as JPMorgan’s bull case still points to about $150,000 by the end of the decade, though with sharp swings along the way.

MEMASA faces a different test, as it needs to turn an AI trading concept into an app people use regularly. If it succeeds, demand for its utility token could grow with the ecosystem, and today’s $0.0155 sale price would become its reference point.

Both Bitcoin’s path and MEMASA’s outcome remain uncertain, and higher potential comes with higher risk.

#5177. Coinbase, Kalshi and Other Firms Back CFTC in Fight Over Stock Perpetuals

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

A dispute over who may regulate perpetual futures linked to individual US stocks is drawing some of crypto’s most prominent companies into federal court. Coinbase, Kalshi, Kraken parent Payward, DRW, Jump Trading and Paradigm have sought permission to support the Commodity Futures Trading Commission in litigation brought by the Capital Markets Alliance Association. Their proposed intervention follows approvals for new stock-linked perpetual products earlier this month.

TL;DR: Six firms filed a proposed friend-of-the-court brief backing the CFTC’s authority in the single-stock perpetual futures case. A judge earlier denied emergency relief but questioned the regulator’s legal basis for the approvals. The court has not delivered a final ruling on whether the products should remain available.

A perpetual futures contract provides economic exposure to price changes without a preset expiration date. It is a familiar structure in cryptocurrency markets. Applying the design to an individual listed US company raises a separate statutory question: which agency’s rules govern the product, and what obligations attach to the exchange listing it?

The Capital Markets Alliance argues that these contracts belong within the securities-law framework rather than under the CFTC’s treatment of security futures. The companies supporting the regulator argue that the law permits the agency’s approach and that blocking the products would limit US-listed alternatives to offshore markets. Those are legal positions advanced in an active dispute, not findings that either side has finally won.

The stakes extend beyond where an order is matched. Classification influences customer protections, margin rules, market surveillance and whether a new product can lawfully be offered to eligible participants.

In the published federal court order, Judge Valerie Caproni denied a request for a temporary restraining order. That means the plaintiff did not obtain an immediate halt at that stage. The reasoning, however, was not a broad endorsement of the CFTC’s authority. The judge indicated the plaintiff was likely to succeed on the statutory question but had not demonstrated the imminent irreparable harm required for emergency relief.

That distinction is essential. A failed emergency application is not the same as a judgment that the products comply with the law. A preliminary-injunction dispute and the broader merits can still produce further decisions, and both sides may submit additional arguments.

NewsBTC recently examined the NYSE’s proposed tokenized securities venue. That project is about representing and settling security ownership. A stock perpetual gives a trader a derivative claim whose value is linked to a share; it does not necessarily transfer ownership of that share or its voting and dividend rights.

Likewise, our coverage of Ripple Prime’s round-the-clock RWA-linked swaps illustrates how conventional markets can be referenced by crypto-native derivatives. The legal treatment of each product depends on its structure and jurisdiction, not simply the fact that it can trade 24 hours a day.

The immediate questions are whether the judge accepts the proposed outside briefs, how the plaintiff’s latest reply addresses the statutory objections, and what relief the court considers appropriate. There is no basis to describe the matter as settled or to assume that every stock perp listing has permanent authorization.

The case is a serious test of how US financial regulation adapts to perpetual trading conventions. Its outcome could influence the shape of future listings, but the facts today support a narrower statement: the CFTC’s approvals are being challenged, and the industry is organizing a legal defense.

Written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5176. XRP, Near Protocol (NEAR), Hyperliquid (HYPE) and Stellar (XLM) Price Analysis for November 10: Market Pressure Keeps Growing

Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

With price currently trading at $1.096, XRP has already recorded another substantial drop. The market positioning of the asset remains weak, as XRP is trading below its major moving averages. Higher lows and fundamental improvements are two main priorities for the asset right now.

XRP's last bullish hope was a short recovery rally towards the $1.34 price threshold. It failed to hold on to that momentum and tumbled towards the area where buyers previously stopped the summer decline. From approximately $1.34, XRP lost roughly 18% of its value and lost everything it built up throughout the recovery.

But where would XRP finally settle down? Realistically, the closest support is the $1.075 level, matching the latest session's low, followed by the $1.03–$1.05 range and the $0.99–$1.00 area, both psychological and technical. A breakdown below, unfortunately, would put the summer bottom under pressure and open the way to further weakness.

But even if XRP is oversold, with RSI below 30, bulls might still stay sidelined. The XRP Ledger activated the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment on November 4. It removed an exploit that, under certain conditions, let accounts authorized to burn an issuer's tokens also mint them. The native supply of XRP has not been affected, luckily, but the amendment is not a factor that can help XRP reach key market levels.

In a case, where bulls somehow wake up, a recovery towards $1.15 and $1.20 is the main priority, while a proper trend reversal would require a move toward the moving averages around $1.30–$1.35.

Whether buyers can defend the $1 level or not is the main test for the asset right now. Without substantial volume, a short-lived price bounce here won't change the situation as a whole, unfortunately.

NEAR entered November under pressure after losing much of its September advance. After briefly reaching approximately $6 in October, the token fell toward $3.66 on November 9, according to the daily NEAR/USDT chart.

That represented a decline of roughly 39% from the local peak and put NEAR back at an important moving-average support zone.

Trading activity has also weakened alongside the price. Volume remains substantially below the levels seen during September’s breakout, suggesting that buyers have not yet returned with comparable strength.

However, lower volume does not necessarily confirm that selling pressure has disappeared. NEAR continues to form lower highs, while recovery attempts remain below its shorter-term moving averages.

Institutional exposure remains part of the investment case. Bitwise’s spot NEAR ETF, launched on September 29, held approximately 15.69 million NEAR worth $58.21 million as of Nov. 5. The fund reported that 99% of its holdings were staked.

Hyperliquid keeps going down and has recently violated an important moving average. It's now trading at $75, going further into downtrend, despite the strong fundamental background around Hyperliquid: Hyperliquid Strategies disclosure and a potential approval by the U.S. futures commission.

The broader market access, however, isn't something that the majority of retail investors consider a bullish catalyst, unfortunately. The asset has not yet found a strong bullish case. It's unlikely to be institutional too.

Technically, the bounce of HYPE/USDT could happen on 200 EMA, especially with a volume uptick and the recovery of the market in general. However, the selling pressure still overweights the buying support, according to exchange flows.

The netflow stays negative, and Hyperliquid is only yet to catch up with the bullish flow of the market. RSI confirms the thesis, moving around 40 and suggesting a bearish momentum, or a sideways movement at least. Volume should also surge substantially.

Key price levels for HYPE are: $72–$73 as the closest support level, $68 as the longer-term moving average, and $78–$80 as the first resistance level. Several moving averages sit at approximately $81–$85, so a breakthrough there should become a foundation for a price recovery.

Stellar already lost an important psychological level of $0.15, and there's a real scenario where XLM breaks down even more.

37% is the current recorded loss from its September peak of $0.235. The correction is rapidly erasing the recovery it has been building and finally invalidating it in October. The price is back in the accumulation range that was present back in May.

XLM moves below all major moving averages, with most of them aiming downwards and hinting at the aggravation of the current trend on the market.

With RSI and volume moving sideways or showing a descent, bear trend keeps the upper hand with sustained pressure on the price going further. The only positive signal could be the fact that RSI temporarily violated the reading of 30, which could be the sign of XLM approaching an oversold zone.

From a fundamental perspective, the Bell Protocol upgrade number 30 is the only thing that could set XLM apart from the rest of the market, with the vote scheduled for November 23.

The update will introduce: muxed contract addresses (ability to serve multiple users from a single smart contract), post-quantum signature verification and more.

In order to make the network more responsive, resilient and stronger in the future, millisecond ledger timing is also coming. It won't make the network faster on its own after the upgrade, but it opens up possibilities for developers to improve the network in the future.

#5174. AI Chip Stocks Bounce After Global Rout—But Is the Bubble Starting to Burst?

AI Chip Stocks Bounce After Global Rout—But Is the Bubble Starting to Burst?

AI stocks staged a comeback Friday after a rout that swept from Wall Street to Asia. But a single green session hardly settles the bigger question for investors heading into the weekend: Is this an overdue correction, or the beginning of an AI bubble bursting?

Nvidia rose 2.78% to $186.64, after plunging 9.3% this Thursday. AMD also bounced to $449.04 following a 17.3% collapse a day earlier. Both stocks remain well below their October peaks, showing how little a sharp relief rally can repair.

It wasn’t just U.S. markets seeing red either. South Korea’s KOSPI lost 6% Friday, while Taiwan’s benchmark dropped more than 8% during the week—its worst week in months. TSMC hit its daily limit-down threshold during the rout. Reuters reported that Taiwanese officials asked state-owned banks to help support prices.

The US Tech 100 cash-market proxy also recovered, gaining 1.85% to 27,007.3, which means it’s down more than 5.5% this week. Its daily candles show the bounce arriving after a much larger slide from October levels, not a return to the previous trend.

The U.S. midterms accelerated fears that Democratic gains would mean tougher scrutiny of data centers. In 40 of 41 local ballot contests on November 3, voters favored limiting development, including by rejecting pro-construction measures, Ballotpedia found. Some votes were advisory, but the political message was unmistakable.

That matters for chip companies because data centers are their biggest growth story. Each project requires vast spending on processors, servers, power and cooling. Restrictions could delay orders or force construction elsewhere.

Yet the doubts predated the election. AI companies and their customers are spending aggressively to build capacity, often before returns are clear. More expensive borrowing makes that bet harder to sustain. Reuters quoted market strategist Steve Sosnick warning that heavily owned stocks were already “priced to perfection.”

A correction means a market has fallen at least 10% from a recent peak. It does not necessarily mean the underlying business is shrinking. Imagine a chipmaker whose shares trade at 60 times annual earnings because investors expect explosive growth. If investors become willing to pay only 40 times those same earnings, the stock can lose a third of its value without profits falling at all.

A bubble bursting is more serious: It happens when expectations prove unrealistic and the money supporting the boom begins to disappear.

History offers a useful warning. During the dot-com crash, the Nasdaq lost roughly 77% between March 2000 and October 2002. The internet was not a fad, but investors had vastly overestimated how quickly companies could turn it into profit.

Telecom operators spent fortunes laying fiber-optic cables for customers who had yet to arrive. Global Crossing, which built an international network, filed for bankruptcy in 2002. The technology survived; many companies and shareholders did not.

AI could face a similar mismatch between long-term promise and near-term returns—and some investors are already talking about it. For example, the RAM shortage is also a business opportunity that many companies are trying to use for their advantage. Former crypto miners are renting their hardware and cloud provider and inference startups are appearing everywhere.

Now, consider a hypothetical company borrowing billions to build a data center packed with Nvidia chips, expecting to rent that computing power at premium prices for years. If customers spend less than anticipated, borrowing becomes more expensive or construction is delayed, the debt still has to be repaid.

The operator may cancel chip orders, its contractors lose work, and utilities that planned new capacity lose anticipated customers. Lenders and investors holding its debt could also take losses. A stock-market selloff can then become a problem for the wider economy.

There is an important difference from 2000, however. Microsoft, Alphabet, Amazon and Meta are profitable businesses with established customers and substantial cash flows, unlike many of the speculative dot-com startups. They can withstand setbacks better than smaller AI-cloud providers that depend on repeatedly borrowing to expand.

It is possible for AI to keep transforming industries while a bunch of individual AI companies fail—and for investors in successful companies to have overpaid.

The financial warning lights are beginning to flicker. Reuters reported Friday that the cost of insuring against a default by AI-cloud provider CoreWeave had climbed above 1,000 basis points. Such protection works somewhat like insurance on a loan: as the price rises, investors are signaling greater concern about repayment.

Yet broader bond markets were still operating relatively normally. That points to growing strain in the riskiest corners of AI financing, not proof of a system-wide crisis.

Next week will offer more concrete tests than another day of red or green candles. Results from CoreWeave, Applied Materials, and Cisco could reveal whether customers are still ordering capacity and equipment, while inflation data will influence expectations for borrowing costs. Stable orders and manageable debt would support the correction argument.

Repeated canceled projects, failed refinancing and shrinking sales would suggest the boom's financial foundations are cracking. The question is not whether AI has a future, but whether every company betting billions on that future can afford to get there.

#5173. Publicly Traded Bitcoin Miner Expands BTC Holdings Despite Market Crash

Publicly traded Bitcoin mining giant BitFuFu, based in Singapore, has massively increased its Bitcoin holdings in spite of the current crypto market crash.

Bitcoin Treasuries data shows that the Nasdaq-listed mining firm, trading under the ticker FUFU, added 66 BTC to its Bitcoin holdings. This brings the total Bitcoin held by the company to a massive 1,454 BTC.

As per Bitcoin Treasuries data, BitFuFu now ranks 38th in the Bitcoin 100 following the recent increase in its Bitcoin holdings.

BitFuFu’s increase in Bitcoin holdings comes at a time when the overall cryptocurrency market has been hit by a massive sell-off in the past few days.

However, it is worth noting that the rise in Bitcoin holdings by BitFuFu is as of Oct. 31, before the recent increase in selling pressure in the crypto market.

The Singaporean mining firm reported that it had 1,388 BTC in its holdings by the end of September in its operational update dated Nov. 6. This means its Bitcoin holdings in October showed a 4.8% month-over-month increase.

Three consecutive months of increases in Bitcoin holdings by the Singaporean Bitcoin mining giant are evidence that the company is not backing down from increasing its holdings in the top cryptocurrency.

BitFuFu’s latest increase in its Bitcoin holdings comes amid a massive drop in the company’s mining output.

In its latest operational report, BitFuFu reported that it mined 137 BTC in October compared to 167 BTC mined in September. This shows that the company’s mining output decreased by around 18%.

Out of the total 137 BTC mined in October, 67 BTC were mined through self-mining, while 70 BTC were mined through cloud mining.

The reduction in mining output by BitFuFu was mainly due to a huge increase in Bitcoin mining difficulty, which increased by around 11.8% in October.

However, BitFuFu was able to increase its Bitcoin holdings while keeping its mining power relatively stable.

In October, the total hashrate managed by BitFuFu was 19.5 exahashes per second (EH/s). This is compared to 19.4 EH/s recorded in September.

BitFuFu Chairman and CEO Leo Lu reiterates commitment to increasing Bitcoin holdings.

#5172. Securitize posts $66M Q3 loss despite 39% tokenized asset growth – Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

BlackRock-backed tokenization platform Securitize has released its third-quarter results of 2026 with revenue and tokenized asset growth but a wider operating loss.

According to the financial results, the company recorded $18.6 million in revenue, up 13% year-over-year (YoY), marking its second-highest quarterly revenue. This happened as the average tokenized assets on the platform reached a record $5 billion, up 39% YoY as of writing.

This comes as the company also agreed to acquire River One to enter institutional asset-backed financing and announced a $50 million share repurchase program, running through December 2027.

This resulted in the firm’s stock rising more than 12% after markets closed on the 9th of November. At press time, $SECZ was trading at $6.97, up by 0.14% in the past trading day.

With this, Securitize also became the second-largest real-world asset (RWA) platform, with $3.7 billion in tokenized asset value, according to RWA.xyz.

In fact, Artemis’s recent report also argued that tokenized equities are in the early stages of a growth path similar to stablecoins. Data scientist Andrew Van Aken at Artemis highlighted how, at around 16 months, tokenized equities sat near $2.9 billion, while stablecoins were closer to $1.2 billion at the same point, and said,

It’s entirely possible tokenized equity market cap could be hundreds of billions of dollars in the coming years.

Echoing similar sentiments, XRP DAT, EvernorthXRP noted,

Tokenization went from $14B to $400B in two years, according to industry estimates.

However, around 90% of these assets remain on-chain primarily as digital records, rather than being actively used in financial transactions.

Speaking at The Korea Herald’s WDAF 2026 event in Seoul, Chief Business Officer at EvernrthXRP, Sagar Shah, highlighted this shift, explaining that the next phase of tokenization will focus on making these assets productive.

The first phase was about getting assets on-chain. The next one is making them productive.

This coincided with AMBCrypto’s recent report, which indicated that AI-related assets accounted for 48% of total tokenized U.S. stock and ETF trading volume, up 4 percentage points from the previous month.

At the same time, traditional financial markets are also moving towards tokenization, with Morgan Stanley integrating DTCC’s new DTC Tokenization Service, which went live on 28th October.

However, all of this happens as the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) is increasing its focus on tokenization as part of its fiscal year 2027 examination priorities. Here, the SEC intends to examine whether companies offering tokenized financial products are following existing securities laws.

#5171. EU Seeks Experts to Help Set Tokenisation Standards as Financial Markets Move Onchain

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: European authorities are inviting nominations for a new Tokenisation Standards Group, with applications due on November 27. The effort is about practical coordination across the financial system, not a new approval for cryptocurrency tokens or a promise that all tokenized assets will be regulated alike.

Europe is trying to solve a problem that has followed tokenized finance from pilot projects into real markets: moving an asset onto a blockchain is only part of the job. Trading, ownership records, custody, corporate actions, payments, and reporting still need to work together.

In a November 9 announcement, the European Securities and Markets Authority said the EU was seeking nominations for a European Tokenisation Standards Group. The deadline for candidates is November 27, 2026, meaning the process remains in its membership-building stage.

A token might provide a fast record of a transfer, but its legal and operational meaning depends on the system behind it. If the asset represents a bond, someone must still ensure that the person receiving it has enforceable rights, that payments reach the correct holder, and that the records remain accurate when a key is lost or a transaction is reversed under applicable law.

Those tasks become harder when different issuers, custodians and trading venues use incompatible methods. Common standards can reduce integration work and make it easier for supervised firms to compare and connect systems. They do not eliminate risk, but they can help clarify who is responsible when something goes wrong.

That is why a coordination effort deserves attention even if it does not announce a flashy new chain or a major token launch. The goal is to make the plumbing of tokenized markets more predictable.

The NYSE’s proposed tokenized-securities venue is an example of how conventional-market institutions are exploring blockchain-based records while maintaining established securities functions. It is a different jurisdiction and project, but it points to the same practical issue: tokenization has to coexist with financial-market rules and infrastructure.

At the same time, Securitize’s work to expand tokenized assets across networks shows why interoperability matters to firms operating in more than one technical environment. A tokenization service that works on several networks can still face fragmented onboarding, settlement and compliance processes.

For European banks, asset managers and infrastructure operators, the payoff from consistent standards would be less duplicated work and more confidence in how instruments move between systems. Whether those efficiencies materialize depends on the resulting proposals and industry adoption.

This announcement should not be read as the EU approving all forms of real-world asset tokens, replacing securities law, or selecting a particular blockchain as a winner. Nor does a call for nominations itself establish binding technical requirements. It is an early process intended to bring stakeholders into the conversation.

Different asset classes could continue to require different handling. A tokenized money-market fund, for instance, has obligations that a privately issued collectible does not. Regulation, transfer restrictions and investor eligibility remain important regardless of how assets are represented.

November 27 is the immediate date to watch. After nominations are considered, the composition of the group and the work it chooses to prioritize will give a clearer indication of what practical standards could emerge.

The significance for crypto markets is less about speculative prices and more about infrastructure. If regulators and financial firms can agree on reliable operating conventions, tokenized finance may have a better chance of expanding beyond isolated tests and into services that ordinary institutions actually use.

#5170. Critical Ethereum Security Alert Issued Over Compromised Software

Blockchain security provider SlowMist has issued an urgent security alert for Ethereum ecosystem users after a complex supply chain attack was detected on Ethersphere's software.

In a recent post on the X social media platform, SlowMist announced that two pieces of software have been compromised by hackers.

The cybersecurity firm claims that attackers were able to break into GitHub accounts and take advantage of legitimate software release processes to deploy malicious software.

The attack could potentially impact users on multiple operating systems, including Windows, macOS and Linux.

The affected software is part of Swarm, a decentralized storage network belonging to the wider Ethereum ecosystem.

Security experts have cautioned that these malicious software releases could enable attackers to compromise cryptocurrency wallet credentials, steal private keys and open up permanent backdoors on affected systems.

One of the most concerning features of this attack is the involvement of Ethereum's blockchain infrastructure in helping manage the malicious software.

SlowMist explains that this malware retrieves and runs malicious JavaScript code.

However, this attack is not just a simple malware infection.

The cybersecurity firm disclosed that attackers are leveraging an Ethereum smart contract as a decentralized command-and-control (C2) configuration repository.

This gives attackers the ability to dynamically update server addresses managing the malware without requiring a new deployment of the malicious software.

Interestingly, the same Ethereum smart contract was previously associated with the infamous SleepyDuck and EtherDuck malware attacks. These campaigns targeted developers using malicious extensions on the Open VSX marketplace.

This points to the possibility that the new attack may be part of a broader operation aimed at compromising cryptocurrency developers and users.

Independent security researchers from Socket and SafeDep also discovered malware aimed at stealing Swarm wallet files and deploying persistent remote access tools.

As a result of this discovery, SlowMist advised users to immediately uninstall the affected package and roll back to the clean version 13.2.0.

In the meantime, users of Swarm Desktop v0.56.2 were advised to update to the clean v0.56.3 software release.

#5169. NYSE Selects Avalanche for Tokenized Stock Settlement: Can AVAX Reclaim $9.00?

Home » Altcoins » NYSE Selects Avalanche for Tokenized Stock Settlement: Can AVAX Reclaim $9.00?

Wall Street’s tokenization push gives Avalanche an institutional boost, but AVAX technicals demand caution at important level.

In a major endorsement of enterprise blockchain infrastructure, the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) has chosen Avalanche (AVAX) technology as the core ledger for clearing and settling trades on its planned tokenized stock platform.

According to an Ava Labs statement, Avalanche will provide the core ledger technology for the clearing firm of the NYSE-affiliated digital trading platform.

Subject to regulatory approval, the platform is designed to enable 24/7 trading, clearing and onchain settlement of tokenized securities.

It will support equities issued across multiple blockchains, while Avalanche will provide the underlying settlement infrastructure, offering predictable performance and costs, controlled network access and regulatory flexibility.

The announcement comes amid intensifying competition in financial asset tokenization. The NYSE has expanded its transfer agent network to six firms, including Broadridge, Computershare and Superstate.

Nasdaq is also developing tokenized securities infrastructure, while DTCC launched its tokenization service in October, using a private Besu blockchain and the Canton Network.

Meanwhile, the SEC’s new innovation exemption is opening the door to tokenized equity trading, with OKX and ICE pursuing a separate initiative.

Following the announcement, AVAX broke out of its previous consolidation range but faced resistance at $8.80–$9.00, triggering a pullback. As of November 10, AVAX is trading at approximately $8.48, up 7.2% over 24 hours.

The rally pushed Bollinger Bands wider, signaling increased volatility. AVAX is now trading below the middle band at $8.62 and the moving-average reference at $8.56, suggesting that upward momentum has cooled.

Resistance stands at $8.60–$8.65 and $9.00–$9.28, while key support can be located around $8.00, followed by $7.70 and $7.30.

Over the next few days, AVAX’s direction will largely depend on whether buyers can reclaim $8.65.A recovery above $8.65 could put AVAX back on course to test $9.00. A break above $9.28, supported by stronger trading volume, would signal renewed bullish momentum.

Failure to regain near-term resistance could leave the token consolidating, while a break below $7.95 would expose lower support levels at $7.70 or $7.30.

The NYSE announcement strengthens Avalanche’s position in institutional tokenization, but its implications for AVAX remain less direct. The platform is expected to run on a dedicated blockchain built using Avalanche technology rather than the public Avalanche network.

The extent of any resulting demand for AVAX will depend on how the platform is implemented and whether it creates mechanisms for value to accrue to the token.

A sustained recovery could put $9.50–$10.00 within reach, with further resistance around $10.50–$11.50. However, failure to overcome overhead resistance could leave AVAX in a broader consolidation range.

The NYSE announcement is a significant development for Avalanche’s institutional prospects, but it has yet to translate into a confirmed bullish reversal for AVAX. After the initial AVAX price rally, traders are now assessing whether buying demand can sustain the move beyond the immediate resistance level.

#5168. Apple Completes the Set: Every Magnificent Seven Stock Opts Out of OKX-ICE Venue

Apple Completes the Set: Every Magnificent Seven Stock Opts Out of OKX-ICE Venue

Every member of Wall Street’s “Magnificent Seven” now wants its stock kept off OKX and ICE’s planned tokenized stock venue.

Apple and Meta Platforms are among 11 companies added to the list of objecting issuers in an updated notice from OKXICE TSV dated Thursday.

They join Microsoft, Alphabet, Amazon, Nvidia and Tesla, completing a sweep by the group of tech giants that makes up a large share of the S&P 500’s value. The other new objectors are Home Depot, Boeing, GE Vernova, Palantir, ServiceNow, S&P Global, KLA, Super Micro Computer and Nebius Group, bringing the total number of companies saying “no” to the OKX and ICE platform to 67.

Meta’s objection carries some irony. The Facebook parent once tried to launch its own stablecoin, Libra, later renamed Diem, before abandoning the project in 2022 amid regulatory pushback.

Apple had been one of the last megacaps missing from the list after Amazon, Tesla, and SpaceX objected in the venue’s Nov. 2 notice. The tally stood at 56 then, and at nine on Oct. 15, when the first objectors appeared.

Just as notable is who hasn’t objected. Crypto-friendly public companies such as Coinbase, Robinhood and Strategy don’t appear on the list. Coinbase launched tokenized stocks for non-U.S. users on its Base network in August. Berkshire Hathaway, Visa, and Exxon Mobil are also absent. It’s unclear whether OKXICE notified those companies.

Under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act failed to advance in the Senate, a venue must notify a company before offering a version of its shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party. If the company objects in writing within 30 days, the venue can’t make that stock available for trading, according to the SEC’s order. It’s worth noting, though, that the objection applies only to that venue.

OKXICE TSV, a 50-50 joint venture between NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange and OKX’s U.S. holding company, plans to offer tokenized U.S. stocks 24/7 through permissioned pools on OKX’s XLayer blockchain. An outside “tokenizer” would hold real shares one-for-one through a registered broker-dealer.

With the market’s biggest names opted out, it’s unclear which major stocks the venue will be able to offer at launch, and its notices don’t list the tokens it plans to make available.

#5166. Securitize Plans River One Acquisition to Connect Stablecoin Capital With Asset-Backed Finance

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Securitize has announced an agreement to acquire River One as it looks to connect tokenized capital with traditional asset-backed financing. The cited $6 trillion refers to the broader market opportunity, not the transaction price, and the proposed deal still needs to close.

Securitize is moving from issuing and servicing tokenized assets toward a potentially larger role in the financing arrangements behind them. The company said on November 9 that it had agreed to acquire River One, positioning the move as a way to connect stablecoin liquidity with asset-backed finance.

The company’s announcement refers to an estimated $6 trillion addressable asset-backed finance market. That figure describes the industry segment it hopes to serve. It is not a disclosure of how much Securitize is paying for River One, or evidence that $6 trillion will move onto blockchains.

Stablecoins can make the cash side of certain transactions programmable and easier to move across compatible systems. Asset-backed finance, meanwhile, involves funding arrangements supported by receivables, loans and other cash-generating assets. Bringing the two together could change how some credit products are funded, serviced and settled.

It could also introduce a set of complications familiar to traditional lenders. A blockchain transfer does not verify the quality of a receivable, establish enforceable collateral rights or guarantee that a borrower pays. Those requirements still depend on underwriting, documentation, servicing and legal controls.

The strategic argument is that a combined platform might handle more of the workflow without forcing investors and originators to stitch together numerous vendors. That remains a prospective benefit, not a completed integration.

Securitize has already been expanding the distribution and technical reach of tokenized investments. NewsBTC previously covered its planned integration with Stellar, which illustrates the importance of access across different blockchains and financial counterparties.

The firm has also worked on high-profile investment structures, including bringing an ARK venture fund onchain. Those initiatives mostly concern representation, issuance or access to an investment. A move deeper into asset-backed financing would focus more heavily on how capital flows through lending products.

That distinction matters because the potential audience widens from crypto-native investors to originators, allocators and institutions looking for operational efficiencies in credit markets. Yet institutional adoption is often limited by risk assessment and compliance, not just transaction speed.

The announcement establishes the parties’ intention to combine, but it does not mean River One has already been acquired or every service is live. Terms, closing conditions and the integration timetable should be checked against subsequent company disclosures rather than inferred from a headline.

Similarly, stablecoin capital is not necessarily a cheap or risk-free funding source. Its usefulness depends on who issues the tokens, how redemptions work, the credit structure and the jurisdictions involved. An institution may still require traditional bank and custody arrangements around the digital component.

The next meaningful updates will be confirmation that the transaction has closed, details on the combined product set and evidence of actual financing activity using stablecoins or tokenized instruments. Transaction volumes and the types of customers involved will tell a stronger story than the size of the theoretical market.

For now, the development signals how tokenization firms are searching for revenue beyond putting an existing security on a blockchain. The harder commercial challenge is turning that capability into financing workflows customers trust enough to use repeatedly.

Bitcoin has been losing wallets on the network since October 16 of this year. With the number of non-empty wallets peaking at 59.99 million that day, the network has already lost roughly 108,700 of them as the price dropped back below $75,000. Smaller holders are clearly running out of patience.

A falling wallet count with the price holding often acts as a sign of sellers getting exhausted, as smaller investors tend to give up when fear is at its highest.

Whether buyers can defend the $74,000 level or not is the main test for the asset right now. The wallet count keeps shrinking, the price is stuck below the moving averages and a short-lived price bounce here won't change the situation as a whole, unfortunately.

The 4-hour chart showed a descending channel in progress.

August’s $0.067 swing low still kept the daily bullish structure alive, while the four-hour and weekly charts looked bearish.

DOGE could still bounce toward $0.071–$0.073 within the channel. If $0.067 gave way, the channel lows and the 23.6% downside extension pointed toward $0.059–$0.060. That would mean a drop of roughly 14%.
