#5161. This Bitcoin Developer’s Idea Would Make Quantum Attacks a Losing Bet

This Bitcoin Developer’s Idea Would Make Quantum Attacks a Losing Bet

Bitcoin developer Cory Fields has an unusual idea for dealing with future quantum thieves: Make them risk their own Bitcoin every time they try to steal someone else’s.

In a November 4 post to the Bitcoin development mailing list, Fields outlined “quantum landmines” and two types of auctions designed to turn attacks on vulnerable wallets into expensive gambles. He called the ideas “thought experiments,” not a completed Bitcoin upgrade.

Bitcoin owners use a secret private key to authorize transactions. A matching public key lets the network check their signatures without learning the secret.

If the whole idea seems too hard to understand, we used AI to generate a video to help you grasp it:

Today’s computers cannot realistically calculate that private key from the public one. A sufficiently powerful quantum computer could use a mathematical technique called Shor’s algorithm to do it, potentially letting a thief spend coins as if they owned them.

Some older Bitcoin outputs have exposed public keys, and certain newer formats reveal them too. Other addresses hide the key until someone spends, so the risk is not identical for every wallet.

Researchers call the hypothetical arrival of a computer capable of these attacks “Q-Day.” No publicly demonstrated machine can steal Bitcoin this way today, and nobody knows exactly when one might exist.

Imagine someone owns 1 BTC in an old, potentially vulnerable wallet. Before Q-Day, this person creates a tiny, separate quantum-safe Bitcoin output containing a hidden proof that they controlled the original private key while quantum theft was still impossible.

Think of it as a sealed ownership receipt. Outsiders can see the small output, but not the evidence tucked inside it, or even that it is a trap.

Now imagine a future thief uses a quantum computer to recover the user’s old private key. Under Fields’s suggested rules, the thief cannot simply take her 1 BTC: They must add 1 BTC of their own from a quantum-safe wallet as collateral.

Both coins enter a restricted output worth 2 BTC. The thief must wait through a challenge period—30 days in Fields’s example—before spending it freely.

The wallet owner notices the transaction and reveals the pre-Q-Day proof. The network then allows the owner to move the full 2 BTC to the quantum-safe address they committed to earlier: their original coin, plus the thief’s collateral.

The attacker has no way to know in advance which old wallets contain these hidden receipts. Even a successful quantum key recovery could become a 1 BTC loss.

The wallet owner would also need matching collateral to initiate a normal post-Q-Day move of her own legacy coin. If they have no spare BTC, the same rule intended to hurt thieves could make moving her savings difficult.

Landmines only work when an owner prepares one before Q-Day and reveals it in time. They cannot protect coins whose keys are already lost, including some of the early Bitcoin holdings widely believed to be inaccessible.

Fields proposes a second mechanism called a staked auction. Before Q-Day, someone could lock up quantum-safe Bitcoin and secretly register a bid against a specific old wallet they expect thieves to target.

Suppose an apparently abandoned wallet holds 50 BTC. A future attacker tries to move it and posts another 50 BTC as collateral; during the auction period, someone who committed a bid in advance can challenge the attempted theft.

In Fields’s example, a successful challenger claims part of the attacker’s collateral while the original vulnerable coins are destroyed rather than awarded to the bidder. The point is to deprive thieves of a reliable payday, not return the coins to a missing owner.

His third idea, a sats auction, requires no advance stake. Instead, a vulnerable-coin spend would trigger a contest in which the highest bidder gets the coins minus their bid, while the runner-up pays their bid and gets nothing.

That resembles a notorious auction game where two bidders keep raising their offers because neither wants to be the person who loses money for nothing. A thief might avoid starting a contest that could cost more than the coins they hoped to steal.

One proposed response to quantum risk is to restrict spending from outdated wallets after a migration deadline. That might prevent theft, but could also permanently lock out legitimate owners who never moved their coins.

Fields is exploring a different trade-off: Keep old coins potentially spendable, but make attempted transfers costly and open to challenges. Bitcoin cannot tell an honest owner from a quantum thief if both can produce the same valid signature.

The trade-off is severe, considering that honest owners might need large collateral deposits, inactive holders could miss their chance to object, and auction rules might burn coins belonging to people who are still alive and simply offline.

Bitcoin would first need a quantum-safe way to hold and spend funds. Wallets would then need to support hidden ownership commitments, while Bitcoin’s consensus rules would have to enforce collateral, waiting periods, special recovery transactions, and auctions.

Developers would also need to agree on when the new rules activate, how much collateral is required, and what happens to losing bids or leftover funds. Fields believes some mechanics could be introduced through a soft fork, a type of Bitcoin rule change, but has not supplied the detailed design.

None of these protections exists in Bitcoin today. Fields has published no implementation or activation schedule, and his original post leaves the auction payouts and even the desired outcome of an auction unresolved.

#5159. XRP Ledger Testnet and Devnet Face Major November Reset Wiping All Data

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP ledger developers have been notified regarding upcoming resets for Testnet and Devnets. According to a statement, XRPL testnet and devnet are scheduled for a reset on November 25, 2026, to improve full history server stability.

Testnet and Devnet for XRP Ledger developers will be reset on Nov 25, 2026 at 5–6 PM PT. All on-chain state (accounts, tokens, AMMs, etc.) will be deleted and will need a re-fund and re-setup afterward. Other networks will continue to operate as usual, including Mainnet, Xahau, and the Hooks Testnet.

Ripple runs Testnet and Devnet, so the community can test out new features and integrations without risking real money.

The XRPL Testnet used to depend on a single full history server, which went down and cannot be recovered without a reset. To avoid this from happening again, an upgrade was performed to increase the server storage capacity.

The resets will delete all ledger data in Testnet and Devnet, including all accounts, transactions, balances, settings, offers, AMMs, escrows, and other data. Testnet will have its Amendment statuses restored to match Mainnet's. However, any AMMs that are re-created after the reset will generally have different account addresses. As a best practice, users are urged not to use the same addresses or key pairs on Mainnet and any test networks.

The important URLs, Network ID, and validator settings for both the devnet and testnets will stay the same. For XRPL users who run an xrpld server that is connected to testnet or Devnet, after the reset they should delete their database data and restart the server.

The last time the XRPL devnet saw a reset was on March 3, 2026. This was necessitated due to the Batch amendment, which required more development at the time and to prevent validators from becoming amendment-blocked, a Devnet reset was required.

The XRPL foundation has launched the XRP Ledger Education Month with up to $40,000 in awards for educators and content creators who explain upcoming amendments like the Lending Protocol or teach how the XRP Ledger works, the role of XRP and the core protocol philosophies.

Since XRPL 3.3.0, memory consumption has been reduced by up to 23% on nodes. Memory growth in XRPL nodes was observed in late summer 2025 and a fix shipped in xrpld 3.3.0.

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP Snaps 92% Of Ripple’s $1.65 Billion Market Swaps

The treasury backed with nearly 500 million XRP tokens uncovers the true reality behind XRP’s RLUSD relation.

That statement differs a lot from the speculation on social media in regards to XRP’s “displaced” status in the ecosystem since RLUSD’s arrival. Ripple’s native stablecoin quickly gained public traction and rose to a $2 billion market cap in less than two months.

It’s crucial to understand that $1.65 billion represents RLUSD’s swap volume on the XRP Ledger’s built-in order-book exchange, rather than the stablecoin’s total circulation across networks. Those figures are considerably larger and don’t necessarily use XRP.

However, the 92% slice on Ripple’s native decentralized exchange vividly paints XRP’s direct involvement in compliance-first stablecoin-dominated markets. That implies the stablecoin’s rapid expansion is accompanied by XRP’s demand, not the opposite.

🚨 Evernorth reports that 92% of $1.65 billion in RLUSD swap volume on XRPL involved XRP pairs, suggesting RLUSD is complementing XRP rather than replacing its role in the ecosystem. pic.twitter.com/PohtD6FWzY

#5157. Slower US inflation leaves Waller’s half-point December hike in play

Core CPI rose 0.3% for a third consecutive month, matching the level Waller linked to considering a half-point December hike before the release.

US monthly headline inflation slowed in October, but core prices rose 0.3% for a third consecutive month, matching the level Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller had associated with considering a 50-basis-point December interest-rate hike.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics reported on Nov. 10 that consumer prices increased 0.4% from September, seasonally adjusted, after September's 0.6% rise. Core CPI, which excludes food and energy, rose 0.3%. The agency did not publish headline or core year-over-year rates.

That leaves a policy tension beneath the slower headline figure. In prepared remarks earlier that day, before the release, Waller said core CPI around 0.3% or higher would warrant considering a 50-basis-point December hike if the report resembled the preceding two monthly readings. He had leaned toward 25 basis points under his expected outlook.

October's core reading matches that approximate level. The comparison does not establish Waller's assessment after the release or a decision by the Federal Open Market Committee.

Prices continued to increase in October, even though the overall monthly pace eased. Energy rose 0.7%, following a much larger 5.2% increase in September. Food prices also increased, with the index rising 0.5% in October.

Shelter increased 0.3% and accounted for more than 30% of the monthly headline increase, according to BLS.

The core result shows why the headline slowdown offers only part of the picture. Removing food and energy leaves the same 0.3% monthly increase recorded in August and September. There was no corresponding improvement in that monthly measure.

Slope chart plots reported September and October seasonally adjusted monthly CPI rates on one linear 0%-0.6% scale. Dashed connector marks an editorial comparison with Waller's approximate core CPI condition, not causation or a policy trigger. Basis points describe the possible rate move, not CPI.

Price changes also varied across categories. Recreation rose 0.8% and motor vehicle insurance increased 0.7%, while used cars and trucks fell 1.2%. Those differences matter when assessing a report that combines slower overall inflation with continuing pressure in several components.

Waller's remarks explicitly state that the views are his own and do not necessarily represent colleagues on the Fed Board or the FOMC.

Waller separately discussed personal consumption expenditures inflation, the measure targeted by the FOMC. The comparison with October's release concerns core CPI; the two measures should not be treated as interchangeable.

BLS did not publish October year-over-year changes for headline or core CPI. During the 2025 lapse in appropriations, the agency did not collect October 2025 survey data, leaving most series without the prior-year indexes needed for an annual calculation.

For Bitcoin readers, the immediate policy question is how officials assess persistent core inflation alongside the slower headline pace.

The next test is policymakers' post-release assessment and subsequent inflation evidence ahead of December. BLS has scheduled November's CPI report for Dec. 10 at 8:30 a.m. Eastern.

#5156. XRP Ledger Introduces Quantum-Proof Security Upgrade to Users

As the XRP Ledger developers continue to make committed efforts to make the network fully quantum-resistant in the coming year, the team has just proposed a major upgrade that has sparked a buzz across the XRP community.

Sparking fresh excitement across the XRP community earlier today, the XRP Ledger (XRPL) Foundation has disclosed plans to introduce quantum-resistant accounts to users.

The team revealed plans to introduce a new feature to the XRP Ledger to offer users strict protection against future quantum computing threats without paying additional transaction fees.

While it is yet to be implemented, the proposed design will allow users to add a NIST-approved ML-DSA-44 signing key to their accounts and disable their existing keys.

This way, it will become harder for potential quantum attacks to compromise accounts, especially those whose public keys have already been exposed.

Apart from the extra layer of protection the upgrade promises to offer users, its cost-effective design has stirred more interest among the XRP community.

Notably, users will not have to pay higher transaction fees to access the additional security, as the proposed feature allows XRP holders to strengthen their account protection without worrying about extra transaction costs.

The proposed upgrade sends relief across the crypto ecosystem as it addresses concerns about the possibility of increasingly powerful quantum computers eventually breaking some of the cryptographic systems currently used to secure blockchains and cryptocurrencies.

While the upgrade is yet to launch, the team revealed that it has scheduled a migration study for the first half of 2027.

The study is aimed at examining how existing accounts can transition to the new security system and what the process would involve for users.

The proposed security upgrade has further sparked more optimism as it is also part of Ripple's broader aim of achieving full quantum protection by 2028.

#5155. Nvidia Board Investigates Jensen Huang After Report He Was Target of Chinese Spy Effort

Nvidia Board Investigates Jensen Huang After Report He Was Target of Chinese Spy Effort

Nvidia's board of directors has opened an independent investigation following reports that CEO Jensen Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, the company said on Sunday.

A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside counsel, will oversee the inquiry. "While the Board has no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised, the Board's inquiry will examine all available information," it said, adding that it "has confidence in Jensen and his leadership team."

We just issued a press release from our board of directors. Read the release: https://t.co/W9yXWJzbkk

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 8, 2026

The investigation follows a Financial Times report on Saturday that a U.S. government memo, written earlier this year based on intelligence information, said the Nvidia CEO had been romantically involved with Alice Huang, an executive at Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed, citing people familiar with the memo. U.S. intelligence believes she was recruited by China's Ministry of State Security, the FT reported. Nvidia declined to comment to the newspaper on the memo. Huang's email to staff, sent on Sunday, did not name the person with whom he had an affair.

Lawmakers from both parties are seeking answers. Republican leaders have asked Huang to appear privately before the House China, foreign affairs and intelligence committees, according to the FT, while Sen. Chris Coons (D-DE), who will chair the Senate Foreign Relations Committee from January, pledged in a tweet that "Congress will investigate and get answers."

The FT's report built on a New York Times investigation last month, which said U.S. intelligence agencies had concluded that Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, a Chinese citizen, was a Chinese intelligence asset, and that surveillance of her had revealed a close relationship with Jensen Huang. President Donald Trump was briefed on the findings roughly a year ago and chose not to act on them, according to the Times.

U.S. officials suspected Megaspeed of helping Chinese companies get around restrictions on advanced AI chips, and the Commerce Department's investigation into the company has been handed to the Justice Department, the Times reported. Megaspeed's sister company in Malaysia bought at least $4.8 billion worth of AI equipment containing Nvidia chips between June 2024 and February 2026, according to trade records cited by the newspaper.

Nvidia told the Times that Huang's interactions with Ms. Huang were "not unusual or unexpected," and that customers like Megaspeed receive "ordinary business information." Megaspeed said it had retained outside counsel to conduct an internal investigation, Ms. Huang did not respond to the newspaper's requests for comment, and China's embassy in Washington dismissed the allegations as "groundless smears and slanders."

In an email to employees on Sunday, Huang apologized to his family and colleagues. "I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did," he wrote, according to a copy published by CNBC.

"Today's news will not slow NVIDIA. Jensen is committed to continuing to lead NVIDIA as CEO without interruption," the company told CNBC.

Nvidia shares were down 5% at $177.32 in premarket trading early on Monday, after closing at $186.64 on Friday, according to Yahoo Finance.

#5154. Upbit lists HYPE – Can Hyperliquid narrow its 45x Spot trading gap?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid has received another major boost, but the impact could be bigger than it initially appears.

Upbit has officially listed HYPE on their South Korean exchange. This is significant, as it means the largest crypto spot exchange in South Korea now holds HYPE. This gives traders in South Korea a massive new pool of liquidity. However, it’s Upbit’s dominance in South Korea that makes the listing even more significant.

According to FP Research, Upbit controlled a significant 68.6% of trading volume across the country’s top five major crypto exchanges in April 2026, much higher than Bithumb’s 19.5%. This gives HYPE access to South Korea’s largest crypto trading platform and a much wider pool of local investors.

Notably, though, Hyperliquid’s [HYPE] expansion into South Korea could be just the beginning.

Recently, Bitso, one of Latin America’s largest crypto exchanges, also added HYPE to its perpetual market. This is interesting since Upbit has a spot listing for HYPE. Put simply, Bitso increases HYPE’s exposure in derivatives, while Upbit provides increased exposure for HYPE to South Korean spot traders.

Together, these listings expand HYPE’s exposure into several new markets and trading segments. However, could the Upbit listing offer more value to Hyperliquid? On-chain activity shows that exposure to South Korea’s large spot market may attract some new buying interest and create increased spot liquidity to help reinvigorate the HYPE ecosystem.

Hyperliquid continues to lead the perp trading market, with competitors still struggling to close the gap.

According to DeFiLlama data, Hyperliquid saw more than $65 billion in perp volume this week alone, compared to Aster’s $11 billion. This shows how much of a commanding lead Hyperliquid has in derivatives. Meanwhile, Q4 perp volume has already reached nearly 50% of Q3’s $620 billion total, suggesting that trading activity on Hyperliquid remains strong despite broader market weakness.

Despite this, there is still plenty of growth left to come. Looking at the chart below, Hyperliquid has been averaging around $15 billion each quarter in spot trading volume this year. However, in Q3 2025 it hit $54 billion.

Looking at the gap between this and the derivatives volume in Q3 2025, which hit around $630 billion in perps, it is safe to say that Hyperliquid’s derivatives volume is almost 45x larger than Spot volume. In essence, Hyperliquid still depends heavily on derivatives activity to drive its on-chain momentum.

Against this backdrop, Upbit’s listing could put HYPE in a stronger position to close that gap. The opportunity to access South Korea’s big spot market opens up new buying opportunities, increasing spot liquidity, and helps diversify Hyperliquid’s trading activity beyond perpetual futures.

If this momentum continues, spot growth could become an important driver of the next phase of Hyperliquid’s ecosystem expansion.

Upbit’s 68.6% market share gives HYPE access to South Korea’s largest spot trading market.

With perp volume nearly 45 times larger than spot volume, Upbit’s listing could bring new buyers, boost spot activity and support HYPE’s growth.

RSI for both assets is moving around 35, so the market isn't oversold yet. With this much DOGE and SHIB sitting on one exchange, selling from Upbit users could add even more pressure on the price, unfortunately.

#5152. AI-assisted firms increase flaw disclosures by 120% with no detected repair slowdown, study finds

A preprint found a roughly 120% relative increase in disclosures against a Chinese-firm comparison group, with no detectable slowdown in the repair channels studied.

Software-flaw disclosures at firms given early access to Anthropic's AI model Claude Mythos Preview rose by roughly 120% relative to the change at Chinese firms without access, a November 4 preprint finds. Measured repair times showed no detectable slowdown.

Thomas Mosk's study finds that AI-assisted bug hunting was associated with more public disclosures without a detectable slowdown in the repair channels studied. Its findings cover publicly disclosed vulnerabilities and specific repair channels, leaving undisclosed workloads and users' installation of patches unmeasured.

Anthropic announced Project Glasswing on April 7, giving selected firms access to the model. The main disclosure analysis compares 13 participants with 267 Chinese firms from October 2025 through September 2026. The Chinese firms could use other AI tools. Selection into Glasswing was not random, and national disclosure practices differ, limiting causal interpretation.

Alongside the 120% relative increase in disclosures, the paper estimates roughly 150% more high-impact disclosures. That category means an above-median impact component in the vulnerability severity score.

Earlier Epoch AI analysis, published July 2, documented a spike in high- and critical-severity disclosures after Mythos Preview. It also warned that public counts omit undisclosed discoveries and can reflect increased interest in bug hunting.

Mosk examines fixing capacity separately, comparing Glasswing vulnerabilities with other non-Chinese vulnerabilities in shorter samples. One analysis follows 658 flaws reported to vendors by the Zero Day Initiative through June 2026. Another covers 14,855 vulnerabilities published through July and tracked by Linux distributions including Debian, Ubuntu, Red Hat and SUSE.

Neither repair channel showed a statistically detectable slowdown. For disclosed flaws, the distributions' median time to a fix stayed at one day. The vendor estimates rule out a slowdown larger than about 13%, although smaller changes remain possible. The downstream estimates still permit an approximately 11 percentage-point decline in the share fixed within 90 days.

For crypto infrastructure, the practical distinction is between finding a flaw, releasing a fix and installing it. CryptoSlate reported on Oct. 29 that Bitcoin Core disclosed three AI-discovered flaws after validation and patch releases. Installation across nodes remains a separate question.

The exploitation results also require care. In a comparison with large non-Chinese firms ending in May, before Glasswing's June expansion, Mosk found no statistically significant change in exploited-vulnerability counts across one-, two- and three-month windows.

The share of disclosed flaws known to be exploited fell in some comparisons. A larger pool of disclosures can lower that share without reducing exploited-vulnerability counts, however. Mosk treats the probability decline as suggestive; the count estimates remain too imprecise to exclude moderate changes in either direction.

#5150. Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

Security measures within XRP’s native ledger ensure no holder on the rich-list gets to take over the chain.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse used the company’s Swell 2026 event to address a long-running claim that Ripple Labs effectively controls the XRP Ledger simply because it holds a large amount of XRP. He said ownership of the token does not confer authority over network rules or validation.

The XRP Ledger currently has more than 150 validators. Of the 35 on the current recommended unique node list, only one is operated by Ripple, roughly 2.9 percent.

Network operators decide which validators they trust and can configure their own lists rather than relying solely on any single published set.

Unlike proof-of-stake systems, XRP holdings do not grant extra voting weight. Independent validators reach agreement on transactions. Protocol amendments require at least 80 percent validator support for two consecutive weeks before they activate.

BOOOM! $XRP Holders🚨🚨🚨 @bgarlinghouse CEO of Ripple debunked one of the biggest misconceptions about XRP ledger!For everyone still claiming Ripple controls the XRP Ledger because it owns billions of XRP, listen to what Brad said at Ripple Swell 2026.Owning XRP does not… https://t.co/zyDgmkPmIM pic.twitter.com/mhpDrqLnXG

Ripple can propose software changes and invest in the ecosystem, but it cannot unilaterally force upgrades.

Three amendments have been activated this year: Permissioned Domains on February 4, Permissioned DEX on February 18, and Batch transactions on October 9, 2026.

Notably, these tools are designed to support more controlled environments for institutional applications while still operating on the shared ledger.

Several highly established financial firms have moved forward with XRP-related products. Aviva Investors launched a tokenized share class of its Sterling Liquidity Fund. Franklin Templeton, DBS Bank, and Ripple have outlined plans involving tokenized fund shares and RLUSD.

Standard Chartered has also announced live institutional custody of RLUSD on the ledger, with additional collateral work planned alongside Ripple and LMAX Group.

These bullish developments do not depend on Ripple controlling the ledger. They rely on the existing consensus rules, low transaction costs, and the ability of independent parties to issue assets and settle on the network.

Even without control of validation, XRP retains specific functions on the ledger. Transaction fees, currently as low as 0.00001 XRP, are destroyed. Funded accounts require a base reserve.

The OG token can also serve as an intermediary asset for bridging different issued currencies when that path offers better execution.

Mr. Garlinghouse’s comments restate a technical distinction that has been part of XRPL design for years: large XRP holdings do not equal governance power.

#5149. Binance Shuts Down Transfers Involving MEXC and Its Affiliates

Just a few days after the U.S. Treasury took action to cut global crypto exchange MEXC off from the U.S. financial system, Binance has also announced strict regulatory actions against the exchange.

On Tuesday, November 10, Binance, the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange by volume, announced its immediate decision to halt all transfers involving MEXC and all its affiliated companies.

Binance released an official announcement earlier today, warning users that it has stopped all trading services related to MEXC following new concerns about its legitimacy.

While the restrictions were announced to take effect immediately, Binance explained that its move follows the recent decision by the U.S. Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) to designate MEXC as a Primary Money Laundering Concern.

As such, Binance has prohibited its users from directly or indirectly sending funds to, receiving funds from, or conducting transactions through Binance involving MEXC and its related entities.

The MEXC-affiliated entities mentioned by Binance include MEXC Global Limited, MEXC Estonia OÜ, MEXC Singapore Pte Ltd, MEXC Global Foundation Ltd, GIDA Technology AG, and several other companies and affiliates operating under the MEXC name.

Although MEXC is among the top 20 crypto exchanges in the world, FinCEN issued an order against MEXC on November 6, cutting the exchange off from the U.S. financial system amid recent fraud allegations.

Notably, the U.S. Treasury accused the exchange of facilitating at least $16 billion in illicit transactions tied to sanctions evasion and cybercrime and of defrauding people of their funds.

While Binance remains the first crypto exchange to take action in accordance with FinCEN’s rule, it appears that many other exchanges are lining up to take similar action.

#5148. Trump Says He Knows 'Nothing' About World Liberty Financial as He Defends UAE Ties

Trump Says He Knows 'Nothing' About World Liberty Financial as He Defends UAE Ties

U.S. President Donald Trump has defended his family's stake in crypto venture World Liberty Financial, saying he knows "nothing" about the firm despite being a co-owner, in an interview after last week's midterm elections.

Asked by columnist Ross Douthat why his co-ownership of a company partly owned by investors linked to the United Arab Emirates government was different from the kind of arrangement he would call corrupt if it involved the Biden family, Trump replied: "Here's the difference [... ] I'm gonna tell you why. Because I know nothing about it," according to a transcript published by CBS News.

Pressed on why he did not simply divest, Trump said most of his money is managed by Wall Street firms through index funds, and that his sons run the family business. "It's hard to say to them, 'Don't do anything anymore for four years because I'm president,'" he said.

When Douthat asked whether foreign investors in the company believe they are buying influence, Trump said: "I don't know." He argued that conflicts of interest have dogged presidents "going back to George Washington," adding that past presidents "own something, whatever crypto was at the time."

"There's crypto, and before that there was oil," Trump said, adding that, "every president has had this problem."

"I'm a bigger name than other presidents, whether we like it or not," Trump said.

World Liberty Financial, which Eric Trump announced in August 2024, issues the WLFI governance token and the USD1 stablecoin. In January, it launched World Liberty Markets, a lending and borrowing platform built on DeFi protocol Dolomite.

Its UAE links have drawn scrutiny from Democrats. Aryam Investment, a UAE-backed entity tied to the country's national security adviser, Sheikh Tahnoon bin Zayed, agreed to buy a 49% stake in World Liberty Financial for $500 million four days before Trump's January 2025 inauguration, Decrypt reported in February, citing The Wall Street Journal. The deal, signed by Eric Trump on World Liberty's behalf, reportedly preceded the administration's reversal of restrictions on advanced AI chip sales to the UAE. A World Liberty spokesperson said at the time that neither Trump nor his Middle East envoy, Steve Witkoff, had "any involvement whatsoever" in the transaction, ABC News reported. Sen. Chris Murphy (D-CT) described the arrangement in February as "potentially criminal conduct." Trump has previously said he was not aware of the deal.

The company's UAE ties have also surfaced in its legal battle with Tron founder Justin Sun. Sun's lawyers asked a federal court to sanction World Liberty, alleging the firm delayed disclosing UAE ownership that could defeat federal jurisdiction over his lawsuit. World Liberty has called Sun's legal campaign a "meritless PR stunt."

The Trump family's financial stake in the venture is set out in World Liberty's own disclosures. DT Marks DEFI LLC, an entity affiliated with Trump and certain family members, owns about 38% of the holding company behind World Liberty Financial, holds 22.5 billion WLFI tokens, and is entitled to 75% of the net proceeds from WLFI token sales, according to the company. A separate Trump-affiliated entity has an indirect claim on interest earned on the reserves backing USD1. The disclosures state that no member of the Trump family is an officer, director or employee of World Liberty, though Trump's sons have remained involved with the company, Decrypt reported earlier this year.

#5147. Bitcoin nears a ‘thinly traded pocket’ – Is $64K next for BTC?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The equities volume on Hyperliquid has overtaken the Bitcoin [BTC] volume once again, Glassnode revealed in a post on X.

Crypto-native traders were chasing the stock market dip. The last time the trading volume ratio climbed to such highs was in August, before the bullish BTC rally from $63k to $87k.

It was appealing to consider that another such rebound was around the corner. However, the past three trading days saw Bitcoin spot ETF outflows of $1.043 billion.

Sentiment simply does not support a recovery. At least, not yet.

The STH to LTH supply ratio was above the upper band and headed higher compared to last week. This meant short-term holders accounted for a greater share of the Bitcoin supply.

In turn, it indicated a market more sensitive to short-term price moves, as short-term holder coins tend to carry thin profits and tend to be moved first under stress.

Glassnode’s Market Pulse report also showed a drop in the percentage of supply in profit. The profitable supply was shrinking, but it has not crossed below the lower band.

Therefore, the market was still in a cooling phase, not capitulation. Moreover, fewer holders were sitting on profits that could tempt them to sell, which should ease the profit-taking pressure.

The Coinbase Premium was negative even when the rally began in August. Its -0.135% reading now meant Bitcoin was cheaper on Coinbase than it was on Binance. The discount had been wider toward the end of October as selling pressure ramped up.

This is considered a signal that U.S.-based investor interest in BTC was low.

The net taker buy-sell imbalance was at -13,142 BTC in the futures market on the 9th of November, reported crypto analyst Axel Adler Jr. The futures imbalance had been at -75,000 on the 5th of November and has contracted since then.

Even so, the overall market regime has shifted away from the bullish signals it displayed a few short weeks ago. Bitcoin was above the 200-day Moving Average at $73,193, but not by much.

The imminent threat was the $64k-$74k pocket. In August, BTC prices had ripped through this area, making it a thinly traded pocket. It could offer little resistance on the way down, too.

#5146. Bitcoin’s projected difficulty cut would still leave older S19 rigs short at 5-cent power

At unchanged Bitcoin price, subsidy and fees, the projected 6.5% difficulty cut would leave modeled 95 and 90 TH/s S19s below electricity coverage at 5 cents/kWh.

Bitcoin miners could earn about 7% more per unit of computing power if a projected difficulty cut materializes, but the relief would leave two older S19 variants needing electricity near 4 cents per kilowatt-hour just to cover machine power.

Hashrate Index’s November 9 snapshot, dated 18:00 UTC, projected a 6.5% difficulty reduction around November 13. The mining data platform put current difficulty at 148.36 trillion and spot hashprice at $31.89 per petahash per second per day. Hashprice measures expected daily revenue from a unit of mining capacity, before expenses.

With Bitcoin’s price, block subsidy and average transaction fees per block unchanged, that projected cut would lift hashprice to about $34.11. It would improve the electricity cushion for miners still operating, while leaving a large gap between older equipment and a more efficient S21.

Lower difficulty increases the probability that a given amount of computing work earns a block reward. Expected revenue per unit of work therefore moves inversely with difficulty when the value of rewards stays fixed.

The scenario calculation divides $31.89 by 0.935, the share of difficulty remaining after a 6.5% reduction. That produces a 7% increase and isolates the retarget’s effect. Actual dollar receipts would also depend on Bitcoin pricing and fees.

Bitcoin recalculates difficulty every 2,016 blocks, using block timestamps to target roughly two weeks. November 13 and the size of the cut remain estimates that can change as more blocks arrive.

The useful operating test is the highest electricity tariff gross mining revenue can cover. Dividing expected daily receipts by the machine’s daily wall-power consumption gives that electricity-only ceiling. A tariff below the ceiling leaves something toward other expenses; a tariff above it means machine electricity alone exceeds gross revenue.

BITMAIN’s S19 specifications list two model 240-Ca variants at 95 and 90 terahashes per second, both drawing a nominal 3,250 watts at 25 degrees Celsius. Its S21 specification lists 200 TH/s at 3,500 watts under the same temperature condition.

The calculations assume continuous operation at nominal ratings and a 6.5% difficulty cut, with Bitcoin price, subsidy and average fees per block unchanged. They exclude pool fees, separate cooling and site loads, repairs, labor, hosting overhead, financing and equipment costs.

#5145. Shiba Inu (SHIB) Scammers Fake 'Ryoshi' to Push 137 Scam Tokens and Dupe Investors

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to longtime SHIB community member Mazrael, since the SHIB deployer wallet went silent in October 2021, at least 137 scam tokens have falsely claimed that the SHIB deployer sent them the coins. The intent of scammers is to 'fake' Shiba Inu's pseudonymous founder, "Ryoshi," and the reason behind this is to appear legitimate: If "Ryoshi" sent it, people trust it.

Mazrael explains how this trick works: a token is just a program, and it keeps its own record of transfers. The scammer can write any address into that record as the sender. So these scammers write the SHIB deployer's address, and on Etherscan it looks like "Ryoshi" sent the token. However, the real SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021.

How scammers fake "Ryoshi" Since the SHIB deployer wallet went silent in October 2021, at least 137 scam tokens have pretended it sent them their coins. Why? To look legitimate. If "Ryoshi" sent it, people trust it. How the trick works: A token is just a program, and it keeps its own record of transfers. The scammer can write ANY address into that record as the sender. So they write the SHIB deployer's address, and on Etherscan it looks like "Ryoshi" sent you their token. But the deployer never signed anything. Click the transaction itself and look at "From". That's the wallet that really sent it, and it's never the SHIB deployer. The numbers: - 9,131+ fake transfers - 2,500+ wallets hit - 0 signed by the deployer Some of the fakes: APE, three "SHIBA INU2.0" tokens, ShibChain, SHIB DAO, METARUFFY, a token literally called "Ryoshi", Caw and Baby Caw, three fake "Shibarium" tokens (a year before the real one), "BONE2.0" and three "UNLEASHED DAO" tokens. Many got shilled by OGs and ex-admins as "Ryoshi-backed" to make them look legit. The real SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021. Always check who signed the transaction, not what the token says.

'The SHIB deployer never signed anything,' Mazrael stated, urging the crypto community to click on related token transactions and examine the "From" address, saying this is the wallet that sent it and was never the SHIB deployer.

Mazrael shared the numbers, outlining over 9,131 fake transfers, over 2,500 wallets hit, and zero signed by the SHIB deployer. He went on to name some of these fake tokens, adding that some of these tokens got promoted as "Ryoshi-backed" to make them look legitimate.

This disclosure warns crypto users to always check who signed the transaction, not what the token says.

In a separate post, Mazrael explained that nothing links the SHIB deployer address to any person.

The SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) only deployed one contract: SHIB, and it hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021. It has no public signed message on record, either on-chain or on Etherscan.

LEASH, BONE, and TREAT each came from different deployers. Mazrael indicated that BONE's deployer is shiba-swap.eth, whereas LEASH came from '0xA221…1494,' the same key that deployed StarLink, whose own site names Woof Decentra as LEASH's developer. That key kept LEASH's rebase oracle, an admin key, for five years.

#5144. Coinbase Joins Circle, Ripple With Final OCC Approval for Federal Trust Bank Charter

Coinbase Joins Circle, Ripple With Final OCC Approval for Federal Trust Bank Charter

Coinbase has received final approval from the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) to charter Coinbase National Trust Company, the crypto exchange said on Monday.

The OCC approved the application last Friday, according to Coinbase. The firm said the charter will let it expand its custody business, which holds digital assets on behalf of institutional clients, under a federal regulatory framework.

"Custody is foundational to our business," the company said, describing itself as the leading crypto custodian globally. The charter will "bring clear and consistent federal uniformity" to its custody operations, it added.

"Trusted custody is essential for bringing more of the financial system onchain," Coinbase CEO Brian Armstrong tweeted on Monday, calling the trust company "another step for building that foundation."

Trusted custody is essential for bringing more of the financial system onchain.

Coinbase National Trust Company is another step for building that foundation. https://t.co/35K2yC3xpj

— Brian Armstrong (@brian_armstrong) November 9, 2026

Coinbase has offered regulated custody since 2018, when its Coinbase Custody Trust Company won a limited purpose trust charter from the New York Department of Financial Services. The new national trust company builds on that foundation while bringing its trust activities into a consistent federal framework, Coinbase said.

The approval comes seven months after the OCC gave Coinbase conditional approval in April, when the company said it was not becoming a commercial bank and would not take retail deposits or engage in fractional reserve banking. Coinbase first applied for the charter last year.

Coinbase joins a growing list of crypto firms with national trust charters. Circle won final approval in July for a trust bank to manage the reserves behind its USDC stablecoin, and Ripple received final approval last month for a trust bank behind its RLUSD stablecoin.

Others are still awaiting final approval. Jack Dorsey's Block has applied for a charter to custody Bitcoin in September, Kraken parent Payward filed its own application in May, and a trust company linked to Trump family-backed World Liberty Financial won conditional approval in August for a charter tied to its USD1 stablecoin, Reuters reported.

The approvals have drawn opposition. Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA) has argued that the OCC's crypto charter approvals, including Coinbase's, are illegal, and the Independent Community Bankers of America has sued the OCC in an effort to stop crypto firms from entering banking through what it describes as a side door.

#5143. Democratic Sweep Puts Crypto Bill On a Cleaner Track

Home » Regulations » Democratic Sweep Puts Crypto Bill On a Cleaner Track

Regaining the majority at the Senate has shuffled the cards for crypto legislation: will Democrats handle it better?

Democrats’ control of both chambers of Congress has raised fresh questions about how soon Washington can deliver a lasting framework for the digital asset industry.

The stalled CLARITY Act and related market-structure legislation now sit before a Congress that is expected to reopen the terms, priorities, and timing of any overhaul.

The CLARITY Act was meant to settle a core industry question: which federal regulator oversees different parts of the crypto market. Its path was already uncertain.

The new congressional balance adds further negotiation over consumer protections, disclosure rules, clearer exchange oversight, and the split of authority between agencies.

Democrats Want to Rewrite the CLARITY Act and Crypto Could Pay the PriceDemocrats are reportedly preparing a major rewrite of the CLARITY Act after their midterm victory.The proposed changes could give the SEC greater oversight of crypto markets. The language, highlighted by… pic.twitter.com/DepyDC7V2J

Democratic majorities are likely to place a crystal-clear emphasis on investor safeguards and enforcement tools before endorsing a broad framework favored by many digital-asset firms.

Quick legislation is not ruled out, but any significant bill that advances could look different and move more slowly than earlier expectations.

Recently, Federal agencies have continued examining registration pathways and rules for crypto trading platforms even as Congress deliberates.

Crypto firms therefore face an evolving patchwork of agency actions while lawmakers work toward a more permanent statutory settlement.

Industry legal observers now see comprehensive market-structure rules crucial for the CLARITY Act potentially slipping into 2027.

Bitcoin’s recent rebound and renewed inflows into spot Bitcoin ETFs show that institutional interest has held up despite the political uncertainty.

Those ETF flows have offered the market some support, yet they do not resolve the regulatory questions still facing exchanges, token issuers, and decentralized-finance projects.

Market participants can already gain exposure through regulated products while much of the underlying industry waits for clearer operating rules.

A longer legislative process would likely preserve that divide, favoring firms able to absorb compliance costs and adapt to agency guidance.

#5141. DeepMind says its AI agents ran a four-day cryptography test with about 20 human messages

The agents coded, ran and debugged a known RSA benchmark over nearly four days, with human approval gates and a large computing budget.

Google DeepMind says two AI agents reproduced a known 1024-bit cryptography computation over nearly four days with about 20 human messages, mostly approvals, carrying the research through coding, large-scale execution and debugging.

The test concerns RSA digital signatures. After billions of advance queries to a simulated signing service, the agents computed a mathematical root needed for a new signature once access ended. They did not recover a private key.

The working paper reports 391 CPU core-years for the completed computation, compared with an earlier team's rounded 1,380 on the same instance and algorithm. That is about a 3.5-fold reduction in measured CPU use. GPU work and preliminary probes are excluded.

DeepMind researcher @pushmeet announced the work on Nov. 6, describing a sandboxed reproduction of a previously solved benchmark instance. The papers say these results do not threaten deployed cryptosystems based on 2048-bit or larger RSA.

The human chose the problem, instance and limits on the signing service, then approved conditions for irreversible steps. According to the paper, the researcher wrote no code, launched no jobs and diagnosed no faults.

A supervisor agent maintained the design, budgets and checks. An executor wrote and tested code, launched distributed jobs and repaired failures. The supervisor also enlisted a specialist sub-agent to accelerate one linear-algebra step.

The agents produced about 33,500 new lines of code, ran 336 distributed experiments and exchanged 218 messages. From design to the verified result, the work took three days and 22 hours.

Their first computed root was wrong because a 32-bit offset overflowed. A correctness check caught the error, and the agents located and fixed it without repeating the advance signing queries. Limited human messaging still came with a human-selected task and approval gates.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

DeepMind reports a two-agent research run with a specialist helper, human approval gates and extensive parallel computing.

The same computation used less CPU time through implementation and execution changes; GPU work and probes are separate.

Design-to-result took 3 days 22 hours; the full-scale-sieving clock took 3.2 days. Large parallel resources explain much of the calendar gain.

About 20 human messages accompanied a supervised, resource-intensive computation with measurable implementation gains.

Authors' working-paper account on a known 1024-bit instance. CPU total excludes GPU work and 7.5 core-years of probes. Calendar comparison mainly reflects parallelism. These figures do not measure total financial cost or demonstrate the new theoretical algorithms.

Source-bound role and feedback diagram. CPU bars share a zero baseline and widths proportional to reported 1,380 and 391 core-years. Reported 3.5-fold ratio is rounded. Time diagrams show two start points; stage spacing is schematic. Native panel switches CPU accounting and elapsed-time views.

Authors' working-paper account on a known 1024-bit instance. CPU total excludes GPU work and 7.5 core-years of probes. Calendar comparison mainly reflects parallelism. These figures do not measure total financial cost or demonstrate the new theoretical algorithms.

The earlier academic computation took about five months. DeepMind's run took 3.2 days from its first full-scale sieving job to the verified root, reaching about 300,000 CPU cores and 16 GPUs at peak. The authors attribute that calendar-time difference mainly to parallelism.

The CPU saving arose from implementation and execution changes within the existing algorithm. The 391-core-year total excludes about 330 reserved GPU-hours, of which about 100 were busy, and 7.5 core-years of probes and rehearsals. The CPU totals also come from different hardware, so this is not a controlled like-for-like comparison.

The cryptographic setup also matters: a separate program holding the modulus factorization answered roughly 4 billion advance queries. It was withdrawn before the target was drawn. The agents then computed the target's root without further queries.

The measured reproduction did not demonstrate the paper's new algorithm for more restricted signing access at cryptographic size. A separate Fan Factories paper estimates roughly 30-fold lower costs per additional 1024-bit target than the multiple number field sieve, after shared precomputation costing about one general number field sieve factorization. These are leading-order heuristic estimates; no fan factory has been implemented.

The working paper identifies the principal models only as A and B and links no public implementation repository, limiting outside comparisons.

#5138. Microsoft-Backed Space and Time Launches Platform to Let AI Agents Verify Private Data

Microsoft-Backed Space and Time Launches Platform to Let AI Agents Verify Private Data

Space and Time, a Microsoft-backed blockchain data project, has launched a platform designed to let businesses use private data in AI tools without handing over the underlying records, the company announced on Monday.

Space and Time Enterprise lets companies prove that calculations made on their private data are correct, so that AI applications, auditors and other stakeholders can check the results without accessing the database itself. Launch partners include onchain asset manager Maple Finance, Indonesian vehicle maker and distributor Indomobil, Oracle software consultancy Trenez and AI data network The Data Foundation.

"Businesses want AI agents to execute workflows using that information, but those agents don't need unrestricted access to the records behind every calculation," Nate Holiday, CEO of MakeInfinite Labs, the original developer of Space and Time, told Decrypt.

The stakes are high, he argued. "The potential cost to a business is the overall value of the business," Holiday said. "Giving AI tools unrestricted access to data creates another avenue of exposure."

Holiday said the company is working with customers in industries including construction, engineering and manufacturing that want to rebuild their systems around AI, so that agents, reporting tools and compliance teams can work from the same business records and check the calculations they rely on.

The platform is built on Proof of SQL, Space and Time's patented technology for proving that database queries were computed correctly. It already works with Google Cloud's BigQuery, and businesses can now run it on their own servers without operating a blockchain, buying specialized hardware or relying on an outside proving service, according to the company.

With GPU acceleration, supported queries over millions of rows can be run and proven in under a second, Holiday said, though actual performance depends on the query, hardware and dataset.

The technology proves that a calculation was carried out correctly on a given set of records, but it cannot tell whether those records were accurate to begin with. "An incorrect entry still needs to be caught through the business's data controls," Holiday said, "but verification gives those controls a much stronger foundation: evidence of which data was used and whether it was processed correctly."

Space and Time raised $20 million in 2022 in a strategic funding round led by M12, Microsoft's venture fund. Last year, Grayscale launched a trust giving investors exposure to SXT, the token that powers Space and Time's blockchain. Earlier this year, the company launched Dreamspace, an AI-powered tool built with Microsoft's Azure AI services that lets users create and deploy onchain apps without writing code.

Space and Time also runs SXT Chain, a decentralized network that supplies verifiable data to DeFi, tokenized assets and stablecoins. Asked whether enterprise customers would create demand for SXT, Holiday said businesses could deploy the product in their own environment, and that the network would come into play as their applications "need to connect to onchain markets," with those workloads paid for through Space and Time.

The company's cryptography is also used elsewhere. Microsoft's Nova project includes Blitzar, Space and Time's cryptographic acceleration library, and the team's work on a commitment scheme called Dory contributed to a16z crypto's Jolt proof system, Holiday said.

#5135. XRP’s Quantum-Resistant Design Comes Into Focus

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP’s Quantum-Resistant Design Comes Into Focus

The foundation behind XRP Ledger aims to mitigate quantum resistance hazards ahead of the practical test.

The chain developers behind Ripple’s native XRP Ledger are actively preparing to implement quantum-resistant measures. For this reason, the devs picked ML-DSA-44, a digital signature standard that the National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST) approved in 2024.

Earlier this year, Ripple introduced a multi-phase roadmap to prepare the XRP Ledger (XRPL) for a post-quantum future, with a target for full readiness by 2028. The new signature format needs 3,732 bytes for public keys and signatures, raising transaction data requirements. This will be audited in mid 2027.

JUST IN: $XRP Ledger unveils quantum-resistant account plan, allowing users to upgrade to quantum-proof security with no additional transaction fees as Ripple targets full quantum protection by 2028. pic.twitter.com/wS2Mk46nG8

As described in Google’s quantum computing research paper, there’s a few key risks the crypto sphere has to handle. The most quoted one is the exposable public key risks when users transact within the blockchain, something that quantum-computing based hackers could take serious advantage of.

There’s a more subtle risk too, one elliptic curve researchers call “harvest now, decrypt later.” Simply put, bad actors can collect publicly visible cryptographic data from the blockchain today and hold onto it. This raises the chances of the account eventually being broken into.

🚨 MASSIVE: THE $XRP LEDGER IS PREPARING FOR QUANTUM COMPUTERS. HERE IS WHAT NOBODY IS TELLING YOU.Every payment you sign reveals your public key.A powerful enough quantum computer could work backward from it.Any account that has ever sent a payment is already exposed.The… https://t.co/hpCLy5Gzzi pic.twitter.com/L32SemqQcE

The extra XRP network storage needed for this kind of upgrade doesn’t necessarily raise the fees. Node operators are absorbing the ~3.6 KB increase per protected transaction (from 105 bytes to 3,732 bytes for key plus the signature), as documented in the proposal. With database overhead, every secure payment would use 5,331 bytes of storage.

David Schwartz Explains What Could Really Drive XRP's ValueFormer Ripple (@Ripple) CTO David Schwartz (@JoelKatz) says using $XRP to pay XRPL (@XRP) gas fees is unlikely to drive its price significantly.He argues that broader network adoption could create value for XRP… pic.twitter.com/X0et9M7El9

The XRP Ledger foundation is planning the inaugural phase of the quantum resistance study in early 2027 as devs polish the proposition of key generation between the Python, JavaScript and server versions. Mitigations are done within a zero-knowledge proof to validate results without disclosing vectors.

Quantum physics may be a tough nut to crack for most crypto currencies, but XRP’s developers are set on a mission to get the chain shielded by 2028.

The release's reconciliation and accompanying quarterly filing separately identify $378.1 million in realized and unrealized gains on digital assets and digital assets pledged, namely Bitcoin and Cronos.

Digital assets and pledged digital assets supplied most of the combined gain pool. Equity-security gains made up the rest; reported net income was $409.7 million.

The $378.1M digital-asset line includes pledged-asset revaluation, other digital-asset revaluation and derecognition gains.

Published unrealized and realized equity gains sum to $118.0M, rounded. The company says gains were driven primarily by bitcoin-related securities.

The $496.1M gain pool combines digital assets and securities. It is distinct from $409.7M in net income.

Realized, unrealized and derecognition gains are accounting categories, not equivalent cash proceeds. The gain pool is not a complete net-income bridge; other income and expenses also enter earnings.

#5133. Don’t Store Crypto on iPhone, Ledger CTO Says

Ledger Chief Technology Officer Charles Guillemet has recently warned cryptocurrency holders not to store critical wallet information on their iPhones, as the security threat is on the rise.

In his recent post on X, the Ledger executive highlighted the fact that vulnerabilities that can leak important data are now becoming more and more accessible for hackers to exploit.

"Unfortunately this kind of vulnerability used to be very expensive and reserved to high potential attackers," Guillemet tweeted.

However, he pointed out that we're now on the verge of seeing the costs of such vulnerabilities go down to zero, meaning that it could become very easy for cybercriminals to exploit them on a massive scale.

"Simply don't store any valuable in your mobile. Especially not your crypto seeds," the Ledger CTO cautioned.

His words came in response to an urgent security alert published by crypto exchange Binance over a vulnerability that was found in Apple's iOS operating system.

In particular, cryptocurrency exchange Binance reported a vulnerability, dubbed worse_query, which can allow malicious applications to gain access to files of other applications installed on the same device.

As specified by the exchange, this security flaw affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2.

This means that crypto wallet recovery phrases, private keys and login credentials can be at risk, as they may be stored in accessible local files.

Binance underscored that this concern isn't exclusive to the exchange's app alone, while adding that the vulnerability can have an impact on the entire crypto space.

The exchange recommended that users uninstall apps that were downloaded from untrusted sources, update their operating systems and avoid installing any unauthorized software.

Binance also advised users to transfer their funds to newly created wallets on clean devices if they think their wallets may have been compromised.

Apple published new security updates on Nov. 9. However, it's not clear if they are enough to fully patch this vulnerability. Still, there have been no confirmed crypto thefts specifically linked to the exploit.

Guillemet's warning comes a month after Ledger's major security incident involving compromised hardware wallets.

Back in October, French cryptocurrency hardware wallet maker Ledger admitted that some hardware wallets distributed by its Southeast Asian reseller CryptoBilis had been tampered with.

It turned out that hackers managed to install complex hardware implants in legitimate Ledger devices. With that, hackers were able to intercept users' recovery phrases when people were setting up their Ledger wallets for the first time.

As shown by Ledger's investigation, malicious implants were capable of capturing the 24-word recovery phrase that appears on the Ledger device screen and then passing it to attackers via a cellular connection.

Blockchain sleuths estimated that this incident might have led to more than $86 million in crypto losses. However, Ledger itself didn't confirm the total amount.

#5129. MARA dumps Bitcoin to erase a $750M collateral risk

MARA repaid its Bitcoin-backed loans, but 2027 bond puts, cash resources and spending choices shape the case for further sales.

Bitcoin miner MARA Holdings has repaid its $750 million of Bitcoin-backed loans, ending the collateral-liquidation exposure attached to those facilities.

Further coin sales could now serve to build cash for bondholder demands, AI infrastructure, and possible share buybacks.

The company's Nov. 9 filing disclosed that it sold 13,597 Bitcoin after Sept. 30 for approximately $1.1 billion and used $750 million to repay the loans in full.

Management's quarterly letter says it intends to use Bitcoin to build cash for AI infrastructure opportunities, convertible-note puts exercisable next year and potential repurchases of its own shares.

As of Sept. 30, MARA had $291.6 million of June 2031 notes carrying a June 4, 2027 cash-put right, and $632.5 million of March 2030 notes carrying a December 1, 2027 right. Together, those September balances total about $924.1 million.

A cash put lets a bondholder require the company to buy back all or part of the notes for cash at face value, plus any applicable unpaid special interest.

The notes' contractual maturity dates remain in 2030 and 2031, and the eventual cash bill depends on which holders exercise and how much principal remains outstanding.

A separate obligation of $48 million of notes outstanding at Sept. 30 matures on Dec. 1. The put calendar does not require MARA to sell Bitcoin to meet potential cash demands.

The 2027 holder-put calendar survives the repayment of MARA's Bitcoin-backed loans. Explore how the potential route to selling changed.

The outstanding Coinbase and Two Prime loans exposed pledged Bitcoin to conditional lender liquidation remedies.

The Nov. 9 disclosures report full repayment after quarter-end. Management still identifies cash uses for continued monetization.

Cash puts are elective holder rights. Repayment removes one liquidation channel while management still plans Bitcoin monetization.
