#5034. Bitcoin Heated Hot Tubs and 278 Washington Bitcoin Merchants : Now Accepting Bitcoin

Washington Bitcoin merchant list has 278 businesses, from coffee shops and salons to a hot tub maker that heats its tubs with bitcoin mining. The list spans 94 named cities, and Seattle leads the way with 46 listings. First up is a homegrown US manufacturing business from Washington that makes a super unique product.

Snorkel has been making hot tubs in the Pacific Northwest since 1979, and its wood-stove business dates to the late 1970s. What makes them special is their full cedar tubs, and the wood stove which is fully submerged into the water of the cedar hot tub and has the “snorkel” coming out of the water. This is very convenient for anyone who has no electrical hook up.

It started with a bet in 2025. Michael Diederich, the owner, began accepting bitcoin for hot tubs after a wager with his brother. Payments were slow to trickle in at first, but bitcoiners kept asking him to build a hot tub powered by bitcoin miners. He started building one, pairing the tub with miners that heat the water. Snorkel works with Dane Soja of Hashrate House on the build.

The prototype with two S19J Pro miners drawing about 8 kW, which it says heated a tub from cold to 104°F (40°C) in under four hours. The project won an innovation award at the Heat Punk Summit in Denver, and a Snorkel tub is on display in Denver. Diederich sees a future where every electric hot tub is heated by miners, because the mining income helps pay back the cost of energy that would otherwise be wasted as heat.

The Rose sits at 226 Main Ave S in downtown Renton, in the Old Renton Pub building. Owner Katie Newman opened it in October 2020 as a gift shop that happened to serve coffee, and the coffee side grew into the larger part of the business. The shop stocks locally roasted coffee, handpicked artisan goods, and grab-and-go food. It won Best Customer Service in the 2025 Best of Renton readers’ poll.

Overcast Coffee is on Capitol Hill, and it started as a walk-up counter at 312 15th Ave E beside Kaiser Permanente. Owner Jonathan Pak opened the first shop there in June 2020. A second location, at 1517 12th Ave inside the Metier bike complex, came later, and the company buys its coffee and ingredients from small, local Seattle businesses.

These 278 businesses come from the BTC Map survey of Washington. BTC Map is community-maintained, please write BTC Map and our series [email protected] if you’d like to see an update.

#5033. MEGA commits $30 million to new products, starting with AI inference

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The MegaETH Foundation rebrands as MEGA and commits $30 million to new products, with an AI inference market set to launch in December.

The MegaETH Foundation, the team behind the Ethereum Layer 2 network MegaETH, has rebranded as MEGA and committed $30 million to new products, starting with an AI inference market planned for December.

The announcement comes after months of work in stealth mode, MEGA said Tuesday, adding that the team will now also develop new products alongside the MegaETH network.

All new products will run on MegaETH, MEGA said. Planned network upgrades, including fast finality, will also help support its AI inference product.

Inference is the process of running a trained AI model to produce an output. MEGA said providers charge different prices for similar services, but buyers may find it hard to know the risks of using cheaper providers they are not familiar with.

"There are still huge gaps between what providers charge for comparable inference," MegaETH co-founder Yilong Li said in a statement. "We think those gaps can be dramatically reduced."

MEGA calls its approach "Purified Intelligence," aiming to make inference from different providers more interchangeable, allowing more suppliers to compete in a global market.

Asked how the market would work and which providers or models would be available, MegaETH co-founder Shuyao Kong told The Block that MEGA had intentionally held back those details until the December launch.

"We believe crypto is the only technology that makes AI fair and open," she said, adding that developers will be the target customers for the inference market.

The $30 million will come from MEGA's existing treasury to fund a range of products, Kong said, noting inference as the project's "largest move."

MEGA said its mandate includes expanding the uses of the MEGA token. When asked what role the token would have in the inference market, Kong said: "This is our mandate, so wherever there are ways to utilize the MEGA token, we will."

The MegaETH team has raised more than $80 million to date across venture funding, community rounds and token sales. That includes a $20 million seed round announced in June 2024, led by Dragonfly with backing from Vitalik Buterin, Joseph Lubin and others; a $10 million Echo round in December 2024; about $13 million from its Fluffle NFT sale in February 2025; and about $40 million from its public token sale in October 2025, according to Kong. She said the public sale amount reflects discounts for buyers who agreed to lock their tokens for one year.

Kong also noted that MegaETH bought back some investors' stakes last year. As The Block previously reported, the team repurchased a 4.75% stake comprising equity and token warrants from pre-seed investors in summer 2025.

The MegaETH network launched its public mainnet earlier this year and currently has about $12 million in total value locked, down from its peak of over $245 million, according to DefiLlama data.

The MEGA token went live in April this year and is currently trading at around $0.05, down over 76% from its all-time high, according to The Block's MEGA price page.

#5031. OpenAI builds kill switch to stop rogue agents within 35 seconds

Control Room cuts off model output for internal research jobs when people or automated monitors flag safety or security concerns.

OpenAI has built a system that can cut off an AI research experiment’s access to model output, following several disclosures of its models acting beyond their instructions this year.

In a blog post Monday, OpenAI called the system “Control Room,” which is a centralized system that tracks model workloads across its research infrastructure and stops them quickly when safety or security concerns arise.

“As our models get more powerful and the associated safety and security risks become bigger, it becomes more and more critical that we can detect potential issues, and quickly stop models in their tracks when needed,” said the company.

The announcement comes after OpenAI disclosed several incidents this year in which research models acted outside their instructions during evaluations. In July, the company said its models had escaped a restricted testing environment and hacked Hugging Face to cheat on a security evaluation.

In October, it disclosed that a research model had used a crypto wallet without authorization to pay for smart contract deployments using testnet Ether, then omitted the spending from its final summary.

Control Room currently covers OpenAI’s internal research workloads, such as training experiments, evaluations and data processing.

It works by assigning each research job an identifier, which the inference engine checks before generating model output. If a person or automated monitoring system orders the job stopped, Control Room revokes its authorization, blocking further output even if the underlying research program keeps running, according to OpenAI.

OpenAI said cached authorizations could delay a shutdown by up to 35 seconds. The system is also designed to reject new requests if Control Room becomes unavailable.

#5029. Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Chainlink and Moody’s Ratings have completed a pilot showing how credit ratings could be beamed across multiple blockchains at once, another sign that the traditional machinery of finance is steadily moving onchain.

Announced Tuesday, the test used Chainlink’s Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol, or CCIP, to distribute Moody’s ratings simultaneously across both permissioned and permissionless blockchain networks.

It ran through the Moody’s Ratings Token Integration Engine, or TIE, the system the 125-year-old ratings giant built to disseminate credit assessments and related data within blockchain environments. The pilot, the companies said, preserved Moody’s existing publication and governance processes while proving the ratings could travel across chains through a single interoperable layer.

The logic is straightforward: as more bonds, funds, and other assets are issued as blockchain tokens, the investors trading them will expect the credit intelligence they rely on to be available wherever those assets live, rather than stopping at any one network’s edge.

Moody’s brings real experience here, having rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds, between January 2017 and October 2026.

“As more financial assets move onchain, credit intelligence has to move with them, across chains and through every rating change,” said Johann Eid, chief business officer at Chainlink Labs, calling the pilot a foundational piece of what institutional capital needs to adopt tokenization at scale.

Fabian Astic, Moody’s global head of digital economy, argued that “the future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure,” and that investors will increasingly expect ratings regardless of the underlying technology.

The effort builds on Moody’s steady push onchain. Earlier this year, the firm launched TIE on the institutional Canton Network before extending it to Solana, making its ratings machine-readable on a major public blockchain.

Chainlink, meanwhile, has positioned CCIP as the connective tissue for institutional tokenization, recently rolling out an upgraded version and reporting tens of billions of dollars in cross-chain value secured as banks and asset managers experiment with moving assets between networks.

The pilot, though, ran entirely on a testnet using representative test data, with no actual rating actions published onchain and no live dissemination involved.

It demonstrated technical capability rather than a launched product, but the companies said the model is designed to accommodate future rating updates under appropriate governance. For now, it’s a proof of concept, albeit one pointing toward a future where even credit ratings are just another piece of data flowing across blockchains.

#5028. Canaan’s Q3 revenue plunges 90% to $14.5 million as bitcoin mining hardware sales slump

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Cryptocurrency and derivatives gains helped the company report an $8 million quarterly profit.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining hardware maker Canaan Canaan Inc. (CAN) sold 1 million TH/s of computing power in the third quarter, bringing in $4.8 million from mining equipment, down 96% from $118.6 million in the same period last year.

Canaan's overall revenue fell 90% to $14.5 million from $150.5 million a year earlier, according to its financial results published Tuesday. Canaan attributed the drop in hardware revenue to lower computing power sold and weaker average selling prices amid reduced market demand.

When Canaan reported its third-quarter 2025 results last November, it had sold more than 10 EH/s of mining machines and produced 267 BTC, The Block reported at the time. The company mined 133 BTC in the latest quarter, generating mining revenue of $9.5 million, compared to $30.6 million in Q3 2025.

Nevertheless, Canaan posted $8 million in net income for Q3 2026, compared with a $27.7 million loss a year earlier amid a $20.1 million fair-value gain on cryptocurrency holdings and another $31.9 million on financial derivatives. However, its operating loss widened to $42.9 million from $23.9 million.

Canaan Chief Financial Officer Jin "James" Cheng attributed the gains to bitcoin's higher price at the end of September. "We recognize that this non-cash gain is distinct from underlying operating performance," he said in the release.

Earlier this year, Canaan acquired Cipher Mining's 49% stake in three West Texas bitcoin mining projects for approximately $40 million. The three projects had a combined installed hashrate of 4.87 EH/s at the end of September, according to the firm.

ERCOT classified Canaan's proposed 1 GW Duval County power project as "Studied Load – Firm" on a conditional basis, and further studies and approvals are still pending. Its second 1 GW Texas project, in Zapata County, remains in early development, the firm said.

Of the approximately 1,875 BTC Canaan held at the end of September, 1,258 BTC was pledged for secured loans. The company also announced a $20 million share buyback program under a $100 million authorization approved by its board on Nov. 6.

#5027. Crypto exchanges need hard dollar caps on hot wallets, CertiK says

“Cold storage is a useful measure of online exposure, but it is not a standalone security score,” the cybersecurity firm told Cointelegraph.

Crypto exchanges should impose hard dollar limits on their hot-wallet holdings rather than relying solely on cold-storage ratios to measure security, according to cybersecurity firm CertiK.

The recommendation came as several crypto exchanges recently disclosed how much they held in cold wallets, seeking to reassure customers after the record Binance hack that targeted its hot wallets. Crypto exchange Gate reported that 88.26% of its total assets were in cold storage, while Coinbase said it keeps more than 99% of customer assets offline.

“Cold storage is a useful measure of online exposure, but it is not a standalone security score,” CertiK’s research team told Cointelegraph.

South Korea requires crypto service providers to keep at least 80% of customers’ crypto assets by value in cold storage. Singapore’s guidelines call for at least 90%, while Japan’s framework requires at least 95% and Hong Kong’s baseline for licensed crypto trading platforms is 98%.

CertiK said that while higher cold-storage levels generally reduce online exposure, the benefit becomes less significant at very high levels.

Related: Analysts see no broader ‘contagion’ from $6.3B Binance hack

Rigid storage ratios could also slow withdrawals, force rushed transfers and concentrate risk in key-management systems.

CertiK said crypto exchanges should consider setting hot-wallet caps for each asset and blockchain according to the strictest of three limits: a percentage of customer liabilities, estimated withdrawal needs and an absolute loss ceiling based on the exchange’s financial resources and recovery capabilities.

“Flexibility should support operating liquidity, while hard dollar ceilings and circuit breakers limit potential loss,” they said.

Binance last week said it had replenished approximately 98% of affected tokens following the hack and would absorb the losses. In a recovery update on Wednesday, the exchange said the remaining tokens were less liquid and would take longer to acquire.

Meanwhile, North Korea-linked hackers have created memecoins to launder funds from the theft, according to Elliptic. The firm traced roughly $430,000 through six newly launched tokens on NEAR, where liquidity pools helped obscure transfers between wallets, though the transactions remained traceable onchain.

#5025. Alta raises $47.5 million Series A to build a blockchain for financial institutions

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Index Ventures led the round, bringing Alta’s total funding to $80 million as it builds a blockchain for financial firms.

Alta, a startup building blockchain infrastructure for banks and payment companies, raised $47.5 million in fresh funding in a Series A round.

The Series A was led by Index Ventures and brings Alta's total funding, including $32.5 million in seed funding, to $80 million.

GV, Blackbird Ventures, Square Peg, and Capital 49 also participated in the round, the startup said Tuesday.

Payments company Airwallex has signed on as an early partner. The companies plan to connect Alta's blockchain to Airwallex's payment network that can handle stablecoin transfers and access to local fiat payment networks.

Alta said its first product will help businesses reduce the cash held in prefunded accounts through just-in-time funding, while allowing them to move idle cash into yield-bearing tokenized assets.

The funding will go toward blockchain development, additional financial partnerships, and hiring engineers and compliance experts. Alta did not disclose a valuation.

#5024. Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic has set a price range of $70 to $78 per share for its initial public offering, according to an updated filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission on Tuesday.

The AI company plans to sell 825 million shares of Class A common stock, 801.4 million of them new shares and 23.6 million from existing shareholders, on the Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," which it revealed when it made its IPO filing public last month. At the top of the range, the offering would raise about $64.4 billion, including the selling shareholders' stake. Underwriters have an option to buy a further 123.75 million shares.

Anthropic expects to price the offering on November 18 and begin trading on November 19, and is targeting a retail allocation of about 20%, well above the norm for an IPO, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the details.

Based on the roughly 13.09 billion shares the filing says will be outstanding after the offering, the range implies a market value of about $916 billion to $1.02 trillion. On a fully diluted basis, the company could be worth up to $1.405 trillion according to CNBC, up from the $965 billion valuation it announced in a funding round in May.

Alongside the IPO, seven of the companies that supply Anthropic's computing power have agreed to buy a combined $26 billion of Class N common stock, which has no voting rights, at the IPO price. At the midpoint of the range, Google and Nvidia would each buy about $10 billion, AMD about $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion each, and TSMC about $500 million, according to the filing. Those shares will not be subject to the usual post-IPO lock-up period.

Anthropic expects net proceeds of about $84.6 billion from the IPO and the private placement combined at the midpoint, which it plans to use for general corporate purposes, including investment in compute and infrastructure, according to the filing.

The company reported revenue of $33.2 billion for the first nine months of 2026, up from $2.5 billion a year earlier. It posted a net loss of $104 billion over the period, most of it from a $94.3 billion non-cash charge tied to the rising value of its convertible notes and other instruments; its operating loss was $1.26 billion. Anthropic has also committed to about $900.9 billion of spending, mostly on compute and infrastructure, of which about $70.7 billion is due within the next 12 months.

Anthropic will list as a public benefit corporation with an unusual governance structure. Its Long-Term Benefit Trust, a body of financially disinterested trustees, will elect a majority of its board, while a single share of Class F stock held by an entity owned by its seven co-founders will carry 50.1% of the company's voting power on most matters, according to the filing. As a result, Anthropic will be a "controlled company" under Nasdaq rules, and holders of its Class A shares will have limited influence over major decisions, the filing warns.

The range comes after a turbulent week for AI stocks. Some major investors told The Wall Street Journal last week that Anthropic's expected $1.5 trillion valuation would likely need to come down following a sell-off in AI stocks, with one suggesting a figure closer to $1 trillion. Anthropic had been eyeing a valuation of around $2 trillion as recently as September, according to an earlier draft filing.

The IPO's order book is already covered at the proposed range, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the matter.
